1                               1. NOTATION AND ECONOMIC DOMAINS                                           1

2
         Dates are 0, . . . , T; B0 = 1 and Bt = ∏ j<t β j with β j > 0. The state domain is               2

3
     invariant under all admissible actions and the declared innovation law. The fixed- 3
4                                                                                                          4
     policy operator is Ttπ v( x ) = ct ( x, πt ( x )) + β t ρt (v( Ft ( x, πt ( x ), Z ))). The Bellman
5                                                                                                          5
     operator is its infimum over feasible current actions. Every cash-invariance and
6                                                                                                          6
     monotonicity statement is restricted to a common domain containing the values,
7                                                                                                          7
     fitted functions, and constant translates used in the comparison. This requirement
8                                                                                                          8
     is substantive for recursive utility.
9
         For compact continuous expected-cost problems with continuous feasible corre- 9
10                                                                                                         10
     spondences, continuous integrals, and measurable minimizers, backward induction
11                                                                                                         11
     produces the Bellman optimum. An adapted policy cannot lower its conditional
12                                                                                                         12
     continuation cost below the Bellman value at any date. Induction therefore proves
13                                                                                                         13
     optimality against history-dependent admissible controls, rather than only Markov
14                                                                                                         14
     grid policies. The same reasoning applies to the stipulated monotone recursive
15
     evaluation when its conditional recursion and measurable selection conditions hold. 15
16                                                                                                         16
     The Gaussian entropic construction in the original article uses separate moment
17                                                                                                         17
     margins rather than compactness.

1      For transport, couple the future economies with the same innovations, starting               1

2    from the same postdecision state. The assumed transition bounds imply pathwise                 2

3    ∥ Xkθ+1 − Xk0+1 ∥ ≤ Lk ∥ Xkθ − Xk0 ∥ + ϵθ,k . Thus ∥ Xkθ − Xk0 ∥ ≤ dk by induction. Each       3

4    future reward differs by at most ηθ,k + Hk dk . Summation with nonnegative weights             4

5    and expectation prove the transport bound. If the recursion is nonlinear but sup- 5
6    norm Lipschitz on a common utility domain, add and subtract the anchor map                     6

7    at the changed continuation to obtain bk ≤ νk + β k bk+1 ; unwind this inequality              7

8    backward.                                                                                      8

9      These proofs depend only on the declared economic perturbation class. They                   9

10   do not assert that all policy changes, altered constraints, or opponent deviations             10

11   preserve the same domain or constants.                                                         11

14     Suppose ℓt ≤ f t − Tt f t+1 ≤ ut and Ttπ f t+1 − Tt f t+1 ≤ ηt for t < T, with terminal      14
                                                                 −1
15   enclosure ℓ T ≤ f T − g ≤ u T . For each date let Bt,k = ∏kj= t β j and set                    15

16                                                                                                  16
                       T −1                               T −1
17                at = ∑ Bt,k uk + Bt,T u T ,        bt = ∑ Bt,k (ηk − ℓk ) − Bt,T ℓ T .            17

18                      k=t                                k=t                                      18

19   We prove Vt∗ ≥ f t − at and Jtπ ≤ f t + bt simultaneously. The terminal claim is the           19

20   terminal enclosure. For the lower induction,                                                   20

21                                                                                                  21

22                Vt∗ = Tt Vt∗+1 ≥ Tt ( f t+1 − at+1 ) ≥ f t − ut − β t at+1 = f t − at .           22

23                                                                                                  23
     For the upper induction,
24                                                                                                  24

25                                                                                                  25
               Jtπ = Ttπ Jtπ+1 ≤ Ttπ ( f t+1 + bt+1 ) ≤ f t − ℓt + ηt + β t bt+1 = f t + bt .
26                                                                                                  26

27   Subtraction yields the theorem. Additive shifts dt change each residual by dt −                27

28   β t dt+1 and the terminal residual by d T ; widths and action gaps are unchanged. This         28

29   proves the invariance without assuming a contraction factor less than one.                     29

30     For retained-policy recertification, new residual enclosures are substituted in              30

31   exactly the same induction. The old actor allowance remains valid because its                  31

32   hypothesis is a uniform statement about the original actor and the unchanged                   32
     4

1    functions. It has no dependence on the mesh on which the new residual happens to          1

2    be validated. The terminal allowance can also be retained. Intersecting two valid         2

3    residual intervals preserves validity and cannot increase width. In the executed          3

4    diagnostics the new residuals are used directly, with the old actor and terminal          4

5    enclosures; the record contains every component needed to reconstruct the bound. 5
6        The zero-shift case does not imply that constant residuals disappear from all         6

7    value brackets. They disappear from the policy-loss bound through upper-minus- 7
8    lower cancellation. Keeping signed endpoints is consequently important for direct         8

9    cost scores as well as for sharp policy bounds.                                           9

                    4. VERIFICATION GEOMETRY AND NUMERICAL ENCLOSURES
11                                                                                             11

12                                4.1. The scalar primitive economy                            12

13       The scalar state is x ∈ [0, 1], action a ∈ [0, 1/4], horizon four, and discount 15/16. 13
14   The transition is                                                                         14

15                                                                                             15

16                  F ( x, a, z) = clip[0,1] {(13/16) x + a + (1/16) x (1 − x ) + z},          16

17                                                                                             17
     with z ∈ {−1/16, 0, 1/16} and probabilities (1/4, 1/2, 1/4). The cost is
18                                                                                             18
                                               2       2       4
19                       c( x, a) = ( x − 11/16) + pa + 4a         + 2(3/8 − x )2+ ,           19

20                                                                                             20

21
     and terminal cost is g( x ) = 2( x − 11/16)2 + 2(3/8 − x )2+ . The two evaluations are    21

22
     expectation and the entropic certainty equivalent ρθ (v) = θ −1 log Eeθv with θ = 1;      22

23
     expectation is θ = 0. The discrete law here is an economic primitive, unlike the          23

24
     continuous uniform law of the coupled experiment.                                         24

25
         For a scalar ReLU network, exact dyadic coefficients permit rational breakpoints      25

26
     and slopes. The largest absolute slope bounds its Lipschitz constant Lt . The Bellman     26

27
     state and action constants used by the verifier are                                       27

28                                                                                             28
             Ltx = 2.875 + (15/16)(0.875) Lt+1 ,           Lta = p/2 + 1/4 + (15/16) Lt+1 .
29                                                                                             29

30   For N + 1 state nodes and A + 1 action nodes, the action cover allowance is Lta /(8A)     30

31   and the residual state allowance is ( Lt + Ltx )/(2N ). Each residual endpoint includes   31

32   the state allowance; the nodal Bellman lower bound includes the action cover. The         32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                         5

1    nearest-node actor allowance adds the selected nodal action gap, action cover, and        1

2    Ltx /N. All operations are outward enclosed.                                              2

3      The terminal residual is piecewise quadratic. Its extrema occur at exact network        3

4    breakpoints, the cost kink, endpoints, or stationary points in each piece. The imple- 4
5    mentation enumerates these candidates using rational arithmetic and rounds the            5

6    extrema outward. It therefore does not replace an exact terminal calculation with         6

7    an uncharged sampled check.                                                               7

       For fixed x, the coupled action objective q( a) is µ-strongly convex on the simplex,
11                                                                                             11
     with µ = 2p − 1/2. For any feasible proposal a and nonnegative multiplier ν, the
12                                                                                             12
     global quadratic lower model yields
13                                                                                             13

                           q(b) ≥ q( a) − ∇q( a)′ a + 2 ∥ a∥2 − νh
                                                        µ
14              inf                                                                            14
             b≥0, 1′ b≤h
15                                                                                             15
                                     1
                                       ∑
                                                                     2
16                                −      min{∇ j q( a) − µa j + ν, 0} ,         h = 1/8.       16
                                    2µ j
17                                                                                             17

18   Indeed, strong convexity gives a quadratic lower model; adding ν(1′ b − h) gives a        18

19   lower bound on the constrained infimum, and minimizing the separable quadratic            19

20   over b ≥ 0 gives the displayed expression. Any nonnegative multiplier is valid. 20
21   Interval lower evaluation and the feasible proposal’s upper value enclose the             21

22   continuous-action infimum without trusting numerical first-order convergence.             22

23     Let δ bound the nodal primal–dual gap, and let L xa bound the change of the             23
                                                                   p
24   action gradient with state. A node action with gap δ is within 2δ/µ of the node           24

25   optimizer. At a state distance r, the gradient perturbation is at most L xa r. The loss   25

26   of retaining the node action is bounded by                                                26

27                                                                                             27
                                                    p        ( L xa r )2
28                                 η = δ + ( L xa r ) 2δ/µ +             .                     28

30   This follows by combining strong convexity at the node with the cross-state change        30

32   sion in the distance to the node optimizer. On the square tensor grid, r = 2/(2N ). 32
     6

1    Rounded acquisition adds its own state-distance allowance before applying the               1

2    formula. The ideal policy and rounded-state policy bounds are separately named in           2

3    the original records.                                                                       3

4        If the gradient of the fitted current continuation has Lipschitz constant Mt and the    4

5    Bellman envelope has gradient Lipschitz constant Ht+1 , bilinear interpolation on           5

6    cells of side 1/N has residual error at most ( Mt + Ht+1   )/(4N 2 ). To see this, condi-   6

7    tion successively on a coordinate and use the one-dimensional linear-interpolation          7

8    error Lh2 /8 for a function with Lipschitz derivative. Summing two coordinate errors        8

9    gives Lh2 /4. The source-bound derivative envelopes, rather than empirical finite           9

10   differences, supply these constants. The terminal allowance uses ( MT     + 18)/(4N 2 ).    10

11       In the retained-policy diagnostic, these residual terms are refined at N = 256, but     11

12   the actor term remains the one certified for N = 128. The record may also contain           12

13   the fine-grid proposed actor’s allowance as part of the inherited verifier output. 13
14   That allowance is not used to claim an unchanged-policy result.                             14

                    4.3. Arithmetic assumptions and implementation boundary                      19

20                                                                                               20

21       The inherited interval implementation applies outward rounding to elementary            21

22   binary operations and encloses its exponential and logarithm approximations by              22

23   explicit series remainders. Stored network and actor coefficients are interpreted as        23

24   exact dyadics. Analytic innovation moments are enclosed using interval arithmetic,          24

25   including cancellation. Exact rational breakpoint calculations and independently            25

26   checked square roots are used where specified.                                              26

27       The resulting certificates concern the defined policies, with numerical enclosures      27

28   around their evaluation. The original rounded-state and execution accounts remain           28

29   separate. The new retained-policy bound does not silently assert that an arbitrary          29

30   hardware implementation with different state acquisition, optimizer, or clipping            30

31   conventions inherits the same target. A deployed implementation must satisfy the            31

32   execution assumptions or add its own allowance.                                             32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                             7

1                 5. PAIRED POLICY COSTS: PROBABILITY AND NUMERICAL ERROR                                          1

2                                                                                                                  2
                         5.1. An unbiased score with certificate-derived range
3                                                                                                                  3
       Under expectation and a fixed policy, the cost score is
4                                                                                                                  4

5
        Zm = f 0m ( x0 ) + ∑ Bt ( Qt ( f tm+1 ; Xtm , πtm ) − f tm ( Xtm )) + BT ( g( XTm ) − f Tm ( XTm )).       5

6                           t< T                                                                                   6

7    Taking expectations cancels the fitted values at every date, including both endpoints. 7
8    The score has expected value equal to actual policy cost even if the networks are                             8

9    poor approximations. To obtain bounded support, write the centered summand                                    9

10   as the nonnegative selected-action gap minus the signed Bellman residual. It lies                             10

11   in [−ut , ηt − ℓt ]. The terminal term lies in [−u T , −ℓ T ]. Thus the score interval has                    11

12   width precisely the corresponding ideal policy bound. Subtracting two scores has a                            12

13   support width equal to the sum of their policy bounds, but its expectation is the                             13

14   direct difference of policy costs. The distinction is that the scores are evaluated along                     14

15   the policies’ trajectories; two regret upper bounds are never used as a surrogate                             15

16   observation of the cost difference.                                                                           16

17                                                                                                                 17

18                        5.2. Continuous innovations through uniform bins                                         18

19                                                                                                                 19
       For each comparison, draw independent integer arrays Kitj uniformly in
20                                                                                                                 20
     {0, . . . , 240 − 1}. A bin represents the continuous interval
21                                                                                                                 21
                                 1 2Kitj          1 2(Kitj + 1)
                                                                
22                                                                                                                 22
                                          −1 ,                   −1 .
                                32 240           32      240
23                                                                                                                 23

24   The exact uniform innovation law can be represented by a uniform bin followed                                 24

25   by a uniform draw inside it. The implementation encloses every draw in that bin                               25

26   and needs no additional within-bin random number. Both policies use the same                                  26

27   innovation bins but propagate their own states and choose their own actions.                                  27

28     At each date and in each coordinate, the actor is the half-up nearest-node rule. If                         28

29   a state interval meets more than one cell, the implementation includes all eligible                           29

30   node actions and propagates their componentwise hull. Such a hull need not itself                             30

31   be a feasible action, but interval evaluation over the hull still encloses every feasible                     31

32   action actually selected; it is used for enclosure, not as a new policy. The bounding                         32
     8

1    arithmetic covers network values, analytic expectations, transition, stage cost, and      1

2    terminal cost. Intersecting sample score enclosures with the deterministic support        2

3    is valid for every bin.                                                                   3

4        For a bin Ki , the computed endpoints Ai , Di are deterministic functions with Ai ≤   4

5    Zi ≤ Di for every within-bin realization. Hence EAi ≤ EZi ≤ EDi . Independence of         5

6    bin draws makes each endpoint sequence independent and identically distributed,           6

7    conditional on the frozen policies. Endpoint widening changes numerical precision,        7

8    not the underlying innovation law.                                                        8

9                                                                                              9

10                                                                                             10
                                    5.3. Simultaneous coverage
11                                                                                             11

12       Let q = 24, α = 0.01, and n = 65536. Scale each endpoint sequence to its deter- 12
13   ministic support of width B. Apply the one-sided empirical Bernstein inequality of        13

14   Maurer and Pontil (2009), Theorem 4, to the lower sequence with reversed sign and         14

15   to the upper sequence in its usual direction, each at tail probability α/(2q). Rescal- 15
16   ing gives the radius in the main article with logarithm log(4q/α) = log(9600). The        16

17   union bound over all 48 tails yields simultaneous coverage at least 0.99. Dependence      17

18   between comparisons is not excluded or required to vanish; only within-comparison         18

19   trajectory independence enters this argument.                                             19

20       The code computes means by outward pairwise summation. If [m, m] contains the         20

21   true sample mean, interval squares of xi − [m, m] bound every squared deviation           21

22   from that mean. Their outward sum divided by n − 1 therefore bounds sample                22

23   variance above. The logarithm uses the inherited interval series. A proposed floating     23

24   square root is checked by exact rational squaring and enlarged until it is an upper       24

25   bound. Since the radius is increasing in variance, range, and logarithm, these            25

26   substitutions preserve coverage.                                                          26

27       This is a probability theorem for the stipulated independent simulation model. 27
28   The fixed PCG64 seed identifies the reproducible realization; it is not a mathematical    28

29   proof that a deterministic sequence is independent. The comparison states, policies,      29

30   sample count, and multiplicity are fixed before the new simulations. No optional          30

31   stopping or policy selection is performed. All 24 intervals contain zero, so no sign      31

32   or equivalence conclusion is made.                                                        32

             6. WORK ACCOUNTS, COMPONENT DIAGNOSTICS, AND REPRODUCTION
12                                                                                           12

13     Operations count Bellman transitions for scalar services and neuron expectations      13

14   for the coupled service; these are different units, not cross-method speed estimates. 14
15   The additional verification clocks include computations of fine-grid action propos- 15
16   als even though those proposals are not deployed. They do not repeat training           16

17   or reconstruct the original service clock. Jobs were scheduled concurrently on a        17

18   resource-limited local host; these clocks describe those diagnostics and cannot be      18

19   read as isolated cross-method speed measurements.                                       19

20     The original records expose state coverage, action coverage, nodal gaps, and          20

21   terminal bounds. A representation/optimizer decomposition is not identifiable           21

22   from them: different parameter errors and fitting trajectories can produce the same     22

23   signed Bellman residual. We report the available components as an error account,        23

24   not a causal identification of each source of approximation error. Training residuals   24

25   are available as separate diagnostics and are not added twice to a certificate that     25

26   already evaluates the trained functions.                                                26

27     A fresh reproduction obtains the two pinned input artifacts, verifies their archive   27

28   digests, and uses a clean result directory. The study commands deliberately refuse      28

29   to overwrite completed records. The deterministic audit reconstructs target counts      29

30   and signed comparisons from all original records, checks retained network and           30

31   actor hashes, and reconstructs every new confidence endpoint from stored moments        31

32   and support. Regression tests exercise interval arithmetic, exact finite-state policy   32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           11

1    inequalities, offset invariance, and source identities. The release manifest binds        1

2    every active source, table, result, and compiled document.                                2

3                                                                                              3

4                                                                                              4

5                         7. RELATIONSHIP TO THE RETAINED THEORY                               5

6                                                                                              6

7      The full controlled-diffusion and viscosity analysis, economic boundary and             7

8    transversality conditions, recursive utility and endogenous-preference models,            8

9    temporal selves, games, quadratic hidden-factor construction, numerical acceptance        9

10   theorems, and historical experiments remain in their unchanged source files and           10

11   compiled companions. The new main article does not purport to re-prove them               11

12   by citing the nonlinear experiment. The preservation map records file hashes and          12

13   LaTeX labels, and the response gives the location of each current answer to the           13

14   referee.                                                                                  14

15     The current revision supplies an integrated paper and new proofs and evidence. 15
16   The new constructive catalogue supplies a separate horizon/price study and re- 16
17   peated isolated timings for its declared backend. An adaptive-grid comparator, a          17

18   nonlinear dimension frontier, and repeated isolated comparisons for the original          18

19   optimizers remain distinct evidence requirements; retained-policy and paired-cost         19

20   results are not substitutes for them.                                                     20

21                                                                                             21

22                                                                                             22

23               8. CONSTRUCTIVE NEURAL POLICIES FROM PRIMITIVE MODULI                         23

24                                                                                             24

25     This section supplies the complete implementation-independent proof of the con- 25
26   structive theorem in the main article. Its assumptions are a compact state set X ⊂ Rd ,   26

27   a common compact action set A ⊂ Ra , a finite horizon, nonnegative discount factors,      27

28   an invariant transition, a common innovation law, and state/action-independent            28

29   monotone cash-invariant conditional evaluation on a common bounded-function               29

30   domain. The primitive moduli are those in the main article’s constructive section. 30
31   Effective compactness and finite certified numerical evaluation are used only for         31

32   finite termination, not for the analytic inequalities.                                    32
     12

1                                    8.1. Bellman moduli and labels                                           1

2                                                                                                             2
       If ∥u − v∥∞ ≤ r, then v − r ≤ u ≤ v + r. Monotonicity and cash invariance give
3                                                                                                             3
     |ρt (u) − ρt (v)| ≤ r. Consequently, when f t+1 is Lt+1 -Lipschitz,
4                                                                                                             4

5                  | Qt ( f t+1 ; x, a) − Qt ( f t+1 ; y, b)| ≤ Lt ∥ x − y∥1 + Lta ∥ a − b∥1 ,                5

6                                                                                                             6

7    where Lt = Cx,t + β t Fx,t Lt+1 and Lta = Ca,t + β t Fa,t Lt+1 . Infima over the same action             7

8    set preserve the state modulus. Compactness and continuity give minima. Alter- 8
9    natively, the same argument uses arbitrarily close minimizers and then takes their                       9

10   error to zero.                                                                                           10

11     Let Ht = Tt f t+1 , qij = Qt ( f t+1 ; xi , a j ), and |qbij − qij | ≤ e. The state and action grids   11

12   have radii h, k. Since every grid action is feasible,                                                    12

13                                                                                                            13

14                             yi = min qbij ≥ min qij − e ≥ Ht ( xi ) − e.                                   14
                                        j           j
15                                                                                                            15

16   Choose an action minimizer a∗ and a grid action within k of it. Then                                     16

17                                                                                                            17

18
                                    yi ≤ qij∗ + e ≤ Ht ( xi ) + L a k + e.                                    18

19                                                                                                            19
     These are bounds on the label array actually produced by the own-future recursion.
20                                                                                                            20
     They do not use Vt∗+1 and do not assume that a regression loss is small.
21                                                                                                            21

22                                                                                                            22

23
                              8.2. Envelope, residual, and selected action                                    23

24     For arbitrary finite labels yi , define Ey ( x ) = mini {yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 }. Each cone is             24

25   L-Lipschitz. For a minimizing cone at x ′ , Ey ( x ) − Ey ( x ′ ) ≤ L∥ x − x ′ ∥1 , and reversing        25

26   the points proves the Lipschitz assertion. If maxi |yi − yi′ | ≤ δ, then every cone shifts               26

27   by at most δ, so ∥ Ey − Ey′ ∥∞ ≤ δ. In particular, numerical label errors cannot inflate                 27

28   the certified slope.                                                                                     28

29     For every node, yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 ≥ Ht ( xi ) − e + L∥ x − xi ∥1 ≥ Ht ( x ) − e. Taking                29

30   the minimum proves the lower residual bound. Choosing a nearest node i ( x ) gives                       30

31                                                                                                            31

32                 Ey ( x ) ≤ Ht ( xi( x) ) + L a k + e + Lh ≤ Ht ( x ) + L a k + e + 2Lh.                    32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                      13

1    If j(i ) minimizes the numerical queries, then                                                         1

2                                                                                                           2
                           qi,j(i) ≤ qbi,j(i) + e = yi + e ≤ Ht ( xi ) + L a k + 2e.
3                                                                                                           3

4    The difference of the chosen-action value and Ht has state modulus at most 2L. 4
5    Thus the nearest-node actor has gap at most L a k + 2e + 2Lh. A deterministic rule for                 5

6    ties among finitely many closed Voronoi cells gives a Borel actor. Any tied nearest                    6

7    node satisfies the same inequality. Because the action set is common, this actor is                    7

8    feasible at every state. This proof would not establish feasibility for an unrestricted                8

9    state-dependent correspondence.                                                                        9

10     At the terminal date, HT = g and there is no action minimization. Labels within                      10

11   eT of g yield −eT ≤ f T − g ≤ eT + 2L g h T . Starting from the terminal network and                   11

12   proceeding backward establishes the assumed continuation moduli at every date. 12
13   The policy theorem, proved earlier in this supplement, then yields                                     13

14                                                                                                          14

15             J0π ( x ) − V0 ( x ) ≤   ∑      Bt (2Lta k t + 4et + 4Lt ht ) + BT (2eT + 2L g h T ).        15
                                        t< T
16                                                                                                          16

17
     The comparison is with the optimum over all admissible adapted policies, not just                      17

18
     policies constant on the grid. The action covering allowance is precisely what makes                   18

19
     the continuous-action comparison possible.                                                             19

20                                                                                                          20
                                 8.3. Realization and finite termination
21                                                                                                          21

22     An absolute value uses two ReLU gates. A sum of d absolute values with affine co- 22
23   efficients computes one cone. The identity min(u, v) = u − max(u − v, 0) computes                      23

24   pairwise minima. A balanced finite tree consumes K cones in O(log K ) stages, with                     24

25   O(K ) additional gates. Positive and negative channels represent signed intermedi- 25
26   ate scalars in an ordinary feed-forward realization. The total number of coefficients                  26

27   is O(dK ); a bounded-fan-in realization can replace each affine sum by a logarithmic- 27
28   depth addition tree. This is an explicit network with weights determined by labels,                    28

29   state nodes, and the primitive upper modulus.                                                          29

30     All constants may be replaced by computable rational upper bounds. The labels                        30

31   can be rational rounded midpoints of certified numerical intervals. State/action                       31

32   covers must be supplied with verifiable covering and feasibility radii, rather than                    32
     14

1    presumed because a set is topologically compact. For the budget s = ε/ ∑ Bt , the              1

2    nonterminal terms satisfy                                                                      2

3                                                                                                   3
                                  2(s/8) + 4(s/8) + 4(s/16) = s,
4                                                                                                   4

5    and terminal terms satisfy 2(s/4) + 2(s/4) = s. Positive-radius covers have finitely           5

6    many nodes and numerical queries reach the specified tolerances by hypothesis. 6
7    Backward construction therefore takes finite work and the policy loss is at most ε. 7
8    This is a deterministic algorithm theorem. It does not turn a finite implementation            8

9    cap into a termination guarantee, and it does not provide a convergence theorem                9

10   for Adam, stochastic gradient descent, or an arbitrary nonconvex loss.                         10

11     The work expression in the article counts all state–action–innovation evaluations            11

12   of the already constructed future network. If numerical precision increases with               12

13   the requested tolerance, the bit cost of each operation and of each weight must                13

14   also increase in the account. A tensor cover retains its exponential state/action              14

15   dimension factors. Analytic or sparse integration can reduce the innovation work               15

16   but does not remove the covering factors merely by being called neural computation. 16
17                                                                                                  17

18                                   8.4. Exact scalar compilation                                  18

19     Let 0 = x0 < · · · < x N = 1 and let the labels be arbitrary rational numbers. Define        19

20                                                                                                  20
                                      zi = min{y j + L| xi − x j |}.
21                                            j                                                     21

22                                                                                                  22
     A left-to-right pass computes the minimum over j ≤ i and a right-to-left pass
23                                                                                                  23
     incorporates j ≥ i. The result is the greatest L-Lipschitz vector lying below the
24
     labels: any such vector w satisfies wi ≤ w j + L| xi − x j | ≤ y j + L| xi − x j | for every j. 24
25                                                                                                  25
     In particular |zi+1 − zi | ≤ L( xi+1 − xi ).
26                                                                                                  26
       For x ∈ [ xi , xi+1 ], every cone centered to the left is represented by the best left
27                                                                                                  27
     intercept and every cone centered to the right by the best right intercept. Therefore
28                                                                                                  28

29                      Ey ( x ) = min{zi + L( x − xi ), zi+1 + L( xi+1 − x )}.                     29

30                                                                                                  30
     For L > 0, the intersection is
31                                                                                                  31
                                   x + x i +1 z i +1 − z i
32                             ri = i        +             ∈ [ x i , x i +1 ] .                     32
                                      2            2L
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                   15

1    When the intersection is interior it is the only additional knot; endpoint intersections          1

2    cause no duplicate knot. When L = 0 the envelope is constant. This proves equality                2

3    of the compiled piecewise-linear function and the defining network everywhere,                    3

4    including non-dyadic intersections and ties.                                                      4

5      For rational knots r j , values v j , and slopes s j , the antiderivative on one cell is        5

6                                                                                                      6

7
                            A( x ) = A(r j ) + v j ( x − r j ) + 21 s j ( x − r j )2 .                 7

8                                                                                                      8
     Rational trapezoidal accumulation computes A(r j ) exactly. Under a continuous
9                                                                                                      9
     uniform innovation on [−σ, σ], the own-future expectation is [ A(b + σ) − A(b −
10                                                                                                     10
     σ )]/(2σ). This is an identity under the original law, not a finite-bin change of the
11                                                                                                     11
     economy.
12                                                                                                     12
       The numerical evaluator encloses each rational coefficient and each native arith-
13                                                                                                     13
     metic operation outward. At a query near a rounded non-dyadic knot, it resolves the
14                                                                                                     14
     cell using the exact rational knot and the exact rational value of the binary64 query.
15                                                                                                     15
     It does not use a rounded knot as a silent change of the partition. An uncertain
16                                                                                                     16
     antiderivative argument is evaluated at its midpoint and expanded by sup | f | times
17                                                                                                     17
     its radius; an uncertain innovation center is expanded by Lip( f ) times its radius.
18                                                                                                     18
     Intersections with [0, 1] discard only rounding overhang when the exact primitive
19                                                                                                     19
     argument is known to lie in that invariant interval. These steps separate analytic
20                                                                                                     20
     integration from arithmetic rounding.
21                                                                                                     21

22                                                                                                     22
                       8.5. Matched spline account and measurement scope
23                                                                                                     23

24     For the spline comparator, linear interpolation of labels with node errors between              24

25   −e and L a k + e gives a residual interval [−e − Lh, L a k + e + Lh]. Indeed the inter-           25

26   polating weights are nonnegative and sum to one, and their weighted distance                      26

27   to the query point is at most h on the uniform one-dimensional mesh. Its width                    27

28   is L a k + 2e + 2Lh, the same form as the envelope width. The nearest-node actor                  28

29   allowance is also the same form. The spline’s actual exact slope, not the neural                  29

30   primitive upper bound, is propagated into its next date’s query modulus. Both                     30

31   terminal certificates conservatively use the primitive terminal modulus. Neither                  31

32   comparator borrows the other’s targets.                                                           32
     16

1      The catalogue records exact rational gap formulas, upper binary64 endpoints,         1

2    all interval-error allowances, defining labels, compiled nodes, and selected actions   2

3    for every rung. It also records evaluated Bellman queries, continuous-law inte- 3
4    grals, antiderivative queries, binary-search upper counts, deployed actor scalars,     4

5    rational storage quantities, maximum stored numerator/denominator bit lengths,         5

6    checkpoint bytes, process peak resident memory, internal clocks, and process           6

7    clocks. Primitive-object counts are not described as a complete floating-point or      7

8    bit-operation count. Peak memory includes the interpreter and warm-up. The inter- 8
9    nal target clock includes failed rungs and durable checkpoint output; independent      9

10   economic comparison is absent rather than costless. These scope qualifications are     10

11   part of the empirical estimand.                                                        11

12     A first-rung timing repetition is not a new random economic environment. For         12

13   each fixed method/horizon/price combination, the three exact certificate arrays        13

14   and deployed objects should coincide; their clocks need not. Equality of object        14

15   hashes is audited separately from timing dispersion. This supplies reproducibility     15

16   for the declared deterministic generator without inferring a probability of success    16

17   for another training algorithm.                                                        17

                        10. WITNESS-PRESERVING POLICY CONSTRUCTION
20                                                                                            20

21     This section establishes the additional policy sandwich and the witness- 21
22   preserving implementation. It uses the same primitive economy, admissible-policy         22

23   comparison and common evaluation domain as the corresponding main-text                   23

24   results. All inequalities concern the continuation actually constructed from its own     24

25   fitted future.                                                                           25

26                                                                                            26

27                                                                                            27
                           10.1. Order comparison and signed accounts
28                                                                                            28

29     Suppose f t − Tt f t+1 ≤ ut and Ttπ f t+1 − f t ≤ dt . Define                          29

30                                                                                            30
                                             j −1
                                                    !                 !
                                      T −1                     T −1
                              Ut = ∑         ∏ βk              ∏ βk
31                                                                                            31
                                                        uj +              uT ,
32                                    j=t    k=t               k=t                            32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                   19

1    with empty products equal to one. The terminal inequality gives VT∗ = g ≥ f T − UT . 1
2    If Vt∗+1 ≥ f t+1 − Ut+1 , monotonicity and cash invariance yield                                      2

3                                                                                                          3
                   Vt∗ = Tt Vt∗+1 ≥ Tt f t+1 − β t Ut+1 ≥ f t − ut − β t Ut+1 = f t − Ut .
4                                                                                                          4

5    The same induction for the fixed-policy map, beginning at g ≤ f T − ℓ T , gives                       5

6                                                                                                          6
                                          J0π ≤ f 0 + ∑ Bt dt − BT ℓ T .
7                                                      t< T                                                7

8                                                                                                          8
     Subtracting proves the one-sided sandwich. Neither ut nor dt is replaced by an
9                                                                                                          9
     absolute value; their signs cancel the effect of arbitrary date-specific changes of
10                                                                                                         10
     origin. The lower loss bound follows from Bellman optimality against all admissible
11                                                                                                         11
     adapted policies. This argument uses no independence of simulations and no
12                                                                                                         12
     linearity of the recursive functional. It is distinct from the expectation-specific
13                                                                                                         13
     paired telescoping theorem retained earlier in this supplement.
14                                                                                                         14
       The old centered certificate is the special bound dt ≤ ηt − ℓt . Its possible slack is
15                                                                                                         15
     due to taking two separate extrema of quantities that share the same fitted value.
16                                                                                                         16
     The new witness result controls their sum directly.
17                                                                                                         17

18                        10.2. Action transport through the minimum circuit                               18

19                                                                                                         19
       At a node xi , the numerical action query attaining yi corresponds to an actual fea-
20                                                                                                         20
     sible action ai . The query enclosure gives Q( xi , ai ) ≤ yi + e. Uniform state Lipschitz
21                                                                                                         21
     continuity of Q(·, a) therefore implies
22                                                                                                         22

23                           Q( x, ai ) − f ( x ) ≤ e + yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 − f ( x )                        23

24                                                                                                         24
     for every node and every state. Selecting an index attaining the minimum cone
25                                                                                                         25
     makes the last three terms sum to zero. Finite minima attain their values, and a
26                                                                                                         26
     fixed ordering of the finite indices defines a Borel selector. The common action set
27                                                                                                         27
     is essential: the proof does not transport an action into a state at which that action is
28                                                                                                         28
     infeasible.
29                                                                                                         29
       Combining dt = et with ut = Lta k t + et + 2Lt ht proves the displayed witness gap.
30                                                                                                         30
     It also implies the separated actor allowance
31                                                                                                         31

32                 Qt ( f t+1 ; x, πtw ( x )) − Tt f t+1 ( x ) ≤ et + ut = Lta k t + 2et + 2Lt ht .        32
     20

1    Thus the earlier numerical separated budget is also a valid, though weaker, bound               1

2    for the new witness actor. Using it as an alternative does not substitute the old               2

3    nearest-node policy. At each nonterminal date, the new sufficient resource choices              3

4    obey s/4 + 2(s/4) + 2(s/8) = s. The terminal bracket is also at most s. Effective               4

5    finite covers and certified numerical evaluation then give finite termination.                  5

6      Only the critic is an affine–ReLU circuit. Returning a comparison index and an                6

7    attached action is a finite measurable algorithm with a possibly discontinuous policy. 7
8    Classical Lipschitz extension and elementary minimum gates remain credited in                   8

9    the main article. The result does not assert their novelty or claim that an equivalent          9

10   conventional envelope implementation is impossible.                                             10

11                                                                                                   11

12                      10.3. Approximate indices and numerical deployment                           12

13
       Let x ′ be an acquired state with ∥ x − x ′ ∥1 ≤ r. Suppose the selected index satisfies      13

14                                                                                                   14
                                                 ′              ′
15                                    yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 ≤ f ( x ) + ν.                               15

16                                                                                                   16
     The cone and the envelope are both L-Lipschitz. Hence
17                                                                                                   17

18                                 yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 − f ( x ) ≤ ν + 2Lr.                            18

19                                                                                                   19

20
     A feasible executed action within distance α of ai adds at most L a α to Q. This proves         20

21
     the stated execution allowance. Rounding a rational switching boundary without a                21

22
     checked comparison can change the selected index; it must be covered by ν or by a               22

23
     certified interval containing all eligible indices. An exact tie presents no difficulty,        23

24
     since both attaining cones give the same certified upper bound. The current records             24

25
     store exact rational boundaries and define the ideal selector. They do not claim a              25

26
     measured deployment performance for arbitrary hardware.                                         26

27                                                                                                   27
                           10.4. Scalar compilation with original witnesses
28                                                                                                   28

29     Let 0 = x0 < · · · < x N = 1 and let the original numerical labels yi be arbitrary            29

30   rational numbers. Define pairs                                                                  30

31                                                                                                   31
            ( pi , li ) = min(y j + L( xi − x j ), j),   (qi , ri ) = min(y j + L( x j − xi ), j),
32                      j ≤i                                         j ≥i                            32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                               21

1    where pairs are ordered first by value and then by index. A forward pass computes              1

2    the first array by comparing (yi , i ) with ( pi−1 + L( xi − xi−1 ), li−1 ); a backward pass   2

3    computes the second. The index always refers to an original label and its action, not          3

4    to a newly regularized value with no action witness.                                           4

5      On [ xi , xi+1 ], the envelope is the minimum of                                             5

6                                                                                                   6
                            pi + L ( x − xi )     and   q i +1 + L ( x i +1 − x ),
7                                                                                                   7

8                                                                                                   8
     with witnesses li and ri+1 . When L > 0 their intersection is
9                                                                                                   9
                                         x + x i +1 q i +1 − p i
10                                   si = i        +             .                                  10
                                            2            2L
11                                                                                                  11
     Unlike an intersection computed after closing both labels, this intersection may lie
12                                                                                                  12
     outside the cell. In that case one affine candidate attains the minimum throughout
13                                                                                                  13
     the cell. An interior intersection divides the cell into exactly two witness regions.
14                                                                                                  14
     If L = 0, compare the two constants directly. The implementation preserves every
15                                                                                                  15
     original node boundary and every interior switch, and chooses the right cell at an
16                                                                                                  16
     exact boundary. Continuity ensures that an adjoining witness remains attaining at
17                                                                                                  17
     the boundary. Thus there are at most 2N witness intervals, with rational endpoints
18                                                                                                  18
     and original action indices.
19                                                                                                  19
       Once rational endpoints are loaded, an ordered search locates a region using
20                                                                                                  20
     a logarithmic number of comparisons. Reading and parsing all stored rational
21                                                                                                  21
     cuts anew costs linear work and is not removed by that comparison bound. The
22                                                                                                  22
     reference accessor used by the tests performs that parsing; the catalogue measures
23                                                                                                  23
     construction and serialization, not repeated policy-query throughput. The record’s
24                                                                                                  24
     binary-search count is only a count of ordered comparisons, not the complete cost
25                                                                                                  25
     of that accessor. Rational numerator/denominator lengths, cuts, witness indices
26                                                                                                  26
     and original actions are stored separately.
27                                                                                                  27

28                                                                                                  28
                       10.5. A common strengthened nearest-node certificate
29                                                                                                  29

30     For a nearest-node policy and any fitted piecewise-affine continuation, let                  30

31                                                                                                  31
                              D = max sup {yi + L| x − xi | − f ( x )}.
32                                    i   x ∈Ci                                                     32
     22

1    The selected-node query and the state modulus give Q( x, π ( x )) − f ( x ) ≤ e + D. On     1

2    each intersection of a nearest-node cell with a linearity interval of f , divided also at   2

3    xi , the expression is affine. Its maximum is attained at an endpoint. Consequently it      3

4    suffices to enumerate the union of the fitted knots, original state nodes and nearest- 4
5    cell boundaries. At a shared nearest-cell boundary both possible node indices are           5

6    included. This is an exact all-state calculation, rather than a sampled maximum.            6

7      Both the cone-nearest ablation and spline-nearest comparator use this calculation         7

8    and their own valid optimal-residual upper bounds. The former has D ≥ 0 because             8

9    the cone envelope lies below every candidate cone. Its witness counterpart achieves         9

10   zero for this particular label-transport upper account. This statement is about the         10

11   account, not an optimality theorem for the actual policy among all feasible controls. 11
12   The spline’s continuation is not replaced by the cone envelope and continues to             12

13   propagate its own exact Lipschitz constant.                                                 13

14                                                                                               14

15                                                                                               15
                        10.6. Evidence identities and scope of reproduction
16                                                                                               16

17     The protocol predates the complete 36-service execution. Fourteen new checks ex- 17
18   amine exact compilation, noisy and dominated labels, ties, finite-policy comparison,        18

19   shift cancellation, common nearest-cell bounds, exact continuous-uniform integra- 19
20   tion in small examples, and immutable numerical dependencies. They supplement               20

21   the analytic proof rather than replace it. The source hashes, all 216 checkpoints,          21

22   and complete process clocks are retained. Formula reconstruction verifies each one- 22
23   sided component, every first crossing, and the identity of the two neural critics in        23

24   the actor ablation. Three repetitions of a deterministic policy are not three economic      24

25   draws.                                                                                      25

28   It identifies the complete R46 source tree as the new review object and retains the         28

23               11. FEASIBLE TRANSPORT UNDER ENDOGENOUS CONSTRAINTS                                  23

24                                                                                                    24
       We give the details underlying the feasible-witness transport theorem in the main
25                                                                                                    25
     article. All norms below are the specified ℓ1 norms. The evaluation functional is
26                                                                                                    26
     the same at the compared state–action pairs. Its monotonicity and cash invariance
27                                                                                                    27
     imply that changing its bounded random input by at most b changes its value by at
28                                                                                                    28
     most b. Thus the primitive state and action inequalities on the feasible graph imply
29                                                                                                    29

30               | Qt ( f t+1 ; x, a) − Qt ( f t+1 ; z, b)| ≤ AtQ ∥ x − z∥1 + DtQ ∥ a − b∥1           30

31                                                                                                    31

32   whenever a ∈ At ( x ) and b ∈ At (z). The proof never evaluates Qt outside that graph. 32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                             25

1                       11.1. The Bellman modulus and noisy feasible labels                       1

2                                                                                                 2
       Fix a ∈ At ( x ). Compactness and the Hausdorff condition give b ∈ At (z) with
3                                                                                                 3
     ∥ a − b∥1 ≤ KtA ∥ x − z∥1 . It follows that
4                                                                                                 4

5                            Tt f t+1 (z) ≤ Qt ( f t+1 ; x, a) + Lt ∥ x − z∥1 .                   5

6                                                                                                 6

7
     Taking the infimum over a and then reversing x, z establishes the claimed Bellman            7

8
     modulus, without assuming that the minimizer is known. At node i, the minimum                8

9
     of exact objective values over its feasible action net is at least the continuous feasible   9

10
     infimum and at most that infimum plus DtQ k t . Taking the minimum of queries with           10

11
     absolute error at most et adds at most et in each direction.                                 11

12
       For every cone, the lower label inequality and the Bellman modulus give                    12

13                                                                                                13
                               yt,i + Lt ∥ x − xt,i ∥1 ≥ Tt f t+1 ( x ) − et .
14                                                                                                14

15   For a nearest state node, the upper label inequality gives an upper bound                    15

16   Tt f t+1 ( x ) + DtQ k t + et + 2Lt ht . The finite minimum has the same bounds and is       16

17   Lt -Lipschitz. The next backward step therefore has precisely the modulus required           17

18   by the construction, independently of the numerical label errors.                            18

19                                                                                                19

20                        11.2. Repair, selection and the dynamic account                         20

21                                                                                                21
       The stored action at,i attains an original numerical query; hence its exact node
22                                                                                                22
     objective is at most yt,i + et . For the minimizing cone, the feasible-pair inequality
23                                                                                                23
     and repair displacement give
24                                                                                                24

25
                             Qt ( f t+1 ; x, Γt,i ( x )) − f t ( x ) ≤ et + DtQ ζ t .             25

26                                                                                                26

27   A fixed ordering of finitely many continuous cone values gives a Borel index selector. 27
28   Composing the finitely many Borel repairs with that selector gives a feasible Borel          28

29   policy. The one-sided sandwich now supplies the discounted loss bound, with the              29

30   original terminal interval unchanged. The full-policy comparison is exactly the              30

31   Bellman comparison assumed in the controlled economy; it is not a comparison                 31

32   restricted to the node action arrays.                                                        32
     26

1      The four nonterminal resource allocations make the bracket at most s/4 + s/4 + 1
2    s/4 + s/4 = s. The terminal bracket is at most s. Effective finite covers, feasible           2

3    action nets, query enclosures and repair procedures consequently give finite termi- 3
4    nation. Where only an approximate repair is computable, its certified displacement            4

5    is charged through DtQ ζ t and its actual action must still be feasible. A numerical          5

6    tolerance for an infeasible constraint violation is not included in this theorem.             6

7                                                                                                  7

8                                 11.3. A transparent exact check                                  8

9
       Let X = [0, 1]2 , b( x ) = ( x1 + x2 )/2, A( x ) = [0, b( x )], and consider the one-date   9

10                                                                                                 10
     objective
11                                                                                                 11
                             Q( x, a) = ( a − 3/4)2 + x1 /8 + x2 /16.
12                                                                                                 12

13   Here AQ = 1/8, D Q = 3/2, K A = 1/2, and L = 7/8 are valid moduli. The exact                  13

14   optimum is attained at min{3/4, b( x )}. For a feasible anchor action, clipping at b( x )     14

15   changes the action by at most |b( x ) − b( xi )| ≤ ∥ x − xi ∥1 /2. The transport theorem      15

16   therefore applies directly, and a reference implementation can compare the exact              16

17   repaired-policy loss with the analytic bound using rational arithmetic.                       17

18     The additional tests independently form feasible node nets and noisy labels,                18

19   compute the cone minimum by direct enumeration, and check the Bellman and                     19

20   selected-policy enclosures on a fixed rational set. They also check the Hausdorff             20

21   modulus, exact feasibility, approximate-repair accounting, nonminimizing-index                21

22   allowances and the zero-constraint-variation reduction. The analytic proof above,             22

23   rather than a finite grid test, establishes the all-state claim. The tests are not training   23

24   data, timing observations, or evidence of a high-dimensional speed advantage. No              24

25   R46 catalogue record is altered by these checks.                                              25

                     12. ACQUIRED STATES AND IMPLEMENTABLE WITNESSES
27                                                                                                 27

28     This section supplies the implementation and statistical details for the new                28

29   acquisition-aware results. The earlier proofs remain in force on their stated do- 29
30   mains. To avoid conflating economic objects, the constrained study and the direct             30

31   scalar comparison are separate experiments: the former constructs new two-state               31

32   policies, while the latter evaluates the actual scalar policies previously constructed. 32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                  27

1                      12.1. Causal information and the one-sided comparison                           1

2                                                                                                      2
       Let Ht contain the primitive history and the observations available before date-t
3                                                                                                      3
     action. The observation process is causal and preserves the stipulated conditional
4                                                                                                      4
     law, or conditional recursive evaluator, for the next primitive innovation. This con-
5                                                                                                      5
     dition permits state errors to depend arbitrarily on the available past. It does not
6                                                                                                      6
     permit a sensor to reveal a future innovation without changing the economic infor-
7                                                                                                      7
     mation structure. The full-information optimum is over feasible policies adapted to
8                                                                                                      8
     this permitted history. Uniform one-step inequalities therefore apply to observation-
9                                                                                                      9
     dependent actions as well as exact-state Markov selectors.
10                                                                                                     10
       Suppose f t − Tt f t+1 ≤ ut , and suppose every permitted observation and true
11                                                                                                     11

18                                                                                                     18

19                                                                                                     19
     actor continuity is used. A date shift f t 7→ f t + st adds st − β t st+1 to ut and subtracts
20                                                                                                     20

1    Combining this with the optimal-residual upper bound DtQ k t + et + 2Lt ht proves         1

4      For a capacity interval, exact repair at the true state has displacement ( ai −         4

5    κ ( x ))+ ≤ K A ∥ x − xi ∥1 . Replacing capacity by a nonnegative certified lower value   5

6    changes the repaired action by at most κ ( x ) − κ ( xb). Downward quantization adds      6

7    less than ∆. These inequalities prove both feasibility and the stated displacement        7

8    bound. In the executed affine-capacity economy the acquisition box has coordinate         8

9    endpoints max(0, xbj − η ) and min(1, xbj + η ). Its minimum capacity is computed at      9

10   the lower corner. All these quantities are dyadic, so that the capacity and downward      10

11   action rounding are exact at the specified resolutions.                                   11

13                   12.2. Finite-precision indices and the identical envelope                 13

14                                                                                             14
       The scalar and two-state experiments store labels on a dyadic lattice. In the
15                                                                                             15
     acquired-state deployment, nodes and measured coordinates have the declared
16                                                                                             16
     dyadic denominators; their coordinate differences and the sum of two absolute
17                                                                                             17
     differences are exactly representable in binary64. No overflow or underflow occurs
18                                                                                             18
     in the evaluated range. For a cone vi = yi + L∥ xb − xi ∥1 , let e
                                                                      L denote the binary64
19                                                                                             19
     slope and vei its computed score. The standard product-and-sum bound, with unit
20                                                                                             20
     roundoff u = 2−53 , gives
21                                                                                             21

                          L − L| + γ2 (Y + d|e
                                             L|) = τ,     γ2 =          ,   Y = max |yi |.
