24   If i minimizes the computed scores and j minimizes the exact scores, then vi ≤            24

25   vei + τ ≤ vej + τ ≤ v j + 2τ. Thus ν = 2τ works regardless of a tie. More general state   25

26   formats must charge the formation of distances too; that cost is not suppressed by        26

27   this dyadic example.                                                                      27

28     An affine–ReLU minimum gate is a − ( a − b)+ . For exact inputs it equals the           28

29   native minimum. Attaching the same index and the same tie rule proves equality            29

30   of the witness actor, including feasible repair. The proof is algebraic and covers        30

31   all states, not only the measurement grid. The scalar implementation’s compiled           31

32   right-cell tie convention is held fixed in this control.                                  32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           29

1         Ordinary rounded ReLU gates need not agree bit for bit with native minima. If          1

2    the exact intermediate minima have absolute value at most M, input errors are at            2

3    most E, and two subtractions implement one gate, a conservative next-level bound            3

4    is                                                                                          4

5                                                                                                5
                                       Enext ≤ E + 6u( M + E)
6                                                                                                6

7    under the preceding finite-range conditions. Indeed the first subtraction contributes       7

8    at most 2u( M + E), the positive-part map is nonexpansive, and the last subtraction         8

9    contributes at most u(3 + 2u)( M + E). The exact minimum is nonexpansive in                 9

10   its two arguments. Iteration from the leaf conversion bound controls a balanced             10

11   circuit. The retained diagnostic checks all 24 scalar witness checkpoints on 1,025          11

12   dyadic points against this budget. Its gate-evaluation clock excludes construction          12

13   and is not a method-level speed comparison. The native representation and the               13

14   ReLU representation share parameters; they are not counted as independent trained           14

15   policies.                                                                                   15

                 13. EXPLICIT CONSTRAINED PRIMITIVES AND RESOURCE ALLOCATION
17                                                                                               17

18        For the new two-state economy, the action lies in [0, 1/4]. Bounding the non- 18
19   negative state terms separately gives F1 ∈ [1/32, 29/32] and F2 ∈ [1/32, 27/32]. 19
20   These bounds already establish invariance before any numerical enclosure is in- 20
21   tersected with the domain. The two state-column sums of the transition Jacobian             21

22   are 11/16 − x2 /8 and 11/16 − x1 /8; every term used in these sums is nonneg- 22
23   ative. The induced ℓ1 state modulus is therefore 11/16. The action column has               23

24   norm 1/2 + 1/4 = 3/4. Capacity has gradient (1/16, 1/16) and hence ℓ1 Lipschitz             24

25   constant 1/16.                                                                              25

26        Let s = (1/2 − x1 − x2 )+ . The first state derivative of stage cost is 2( x1 − 5/8) + 26
27   ( x1 − x2 )/2 − 4s; the second is the symmetric expression. On each of the two short- 27
28   age regions these derivatives are affine. At the vertices of the resulting polygons         28

29   their absolute values are at most 13/4. Doubling the squared-target part changes            29

30   this bound to 9/2 for terminal cost. The action derivative 2pa + 16a3 is nonnega- 30
31   tive and at most p/2 + 1/4. These are constants for the primitive equations, not            31

32   estimates from the evaluation grid.                                                         32
     30

1      For a uniform tensor grid, each coordinate is within 1/(2N ) of a node, giving           1

2    h = 1/N. The maximum feasible interval length is 1/4, so a uniform N-subdivision           2

3    feasible net has radius at most 1/(8N ). At different states the capacity intervals        3

4    have Hausdorff distance at most (1/16)∥ x − x ′ ∥1 . The map a 7→ min{ a, κ ( x )} is      4

5    measurable and feasible, with the displacement bound proved above. Thus neither            5

6    an unspecified covering oracle nor an unspecified feasible projection is used here.        6

7      A shock cell has length 1/(16M). Its conditional mean distance to the midpoint is        7

8    1/(64M ). The transition’s shock derivative is (1, −1), of ℓ1 norm two. Consequently       8

9    a future continuation of modulus L has midpoint expectation error at most L/(32M). 9
10   The program encloses numerical evaluation at all midpoints, averages outward,              10

11   adds this continuous-law remainder, and discounts it. Labels are dyadic rounded            11

12   centers with an error enclosing the full numerical query. The economic law remains         12

13   continuous; this is not a proof for a substituted finite-state shock process.              13

14                                                                                              14

15                         13.1. The strengthened bilinear comparator                           15

16                                                                                              16
       Write λi ( x ) for the four bilinear interpolation weights in a tensor cell. They are
17                                                                                              17
     nonnegative, sum to one and reproduce the two coordinates. In one coordinate,
18                                                                                              18
     at relative position q ∈ [0, 1], the weighted distance to the two endpoints is 2q(1 −
19                                                                                              19
     q)/N ≤ 1/(2N ). Adding coordinates gives ∑i λi ( x )∥ x − xi ∥1 ≤ 1/N. If the nodal
20                                                                                              20
     Bellman labels have lower error −et and upper error DtQ k t + et , the graph-Lipschitz
21                                                                                              21
     Bellman target therefore obeys
22                                                                                              22

23                  −et − Lt /N ≤ f t ( x ) − Tt f t+1 ( x ) ≤ DtQ k t + et + Lt /N.            23

24                                                                                              24

25   The interpolant’s own Lipschitz modulus is N times the largest adjacent nodal              25

26   difference in absolute value. Each partial derivative is a convex combination of two       26

27   adjacent differences. In this execution the differences are dyadic with sufficiently few   27

28   significant bits to be represented exactly; the computed modulus is consequently           28

29   an upper bound without a hidden downward rounding.                                         29

30     For the nearest-node actor, each closed Voronoi cell is split at its node coordi- 30
31   nates. On each of the at most four resulting rectangles the absolute-distance term is      31

32   affine, and the continuation is a single bilinear polynomial. Fixing one coordinate        32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           31

1    makes the difference affine in the other. Successively maximizing both coordinates        1

2    therefore attains a maximum at a vertex. The union contains at most nine points. 2
3    Evaluating every point outward bounds the complete supremum, including bound- 3
4    ary ties. This proves the selected-policy allowance used by the comparator rather         4

5    than comparing a sharpened witness bound to an unnecessarily separated baseline. 5
6                                                                                              6

7                           13.2. Sufficient caps versus executed caps                         7

8      For the cone generator,                                                                 8

9                                                                                              9

10                          Lt = Cx + K A Ca + β( Fx + K A Fa ) Lt+1 .                         10

11                                                                                             11
     Here b = β( Fx + K A Fa ) = 705/1024 < 1, giving the horizon-uniform bound L =
12                                                                                             12
     max{ L g , (Cx + K A Ca )/(1 − b)}. The action modulus is at most D = Ca + βFa L.
13                                                                                             13
     With M = N, nonterminal label error is at most βL/(32N ) + ξ. Substituting into the
14                                                                                             14
     one-sided loss bound yields exactly
15                                                                                             15
                                                        T
16                       ST ( D/8 + 2L + βL/16) + 2β L g                                       16
                  GN ≤                                   + 2ST ξ + 2β T ξ T .
17                                      N                                                      17

18   Choosing the spatial and numerical allocations as in the article proves the suffi- 18
19   cient cap. It is not a statement that the fixed ladder 4, 8, 16 contains that cap, nor    19

20   that the labels rounded to a fixed lattice support arbitrary tolerances. Acquisition,     20

21   quantization and selector excess are additional summands in the same budget.              21

22     There are ( N + 1)2 nodes, N + 1 feasible actions per node, N shock midpoints           22

23   per query and T backward dates. A direct cone query evaluates ( N + 1)2 pieces,           23

24   while a bilinear query evaluates one local patch. The resulting operation bounds          24

25   are O( TN 6 ) and O( TN 4 ), respectively. These formulas include label formation,        25

26   not only verification. Actor storage and critic-label storage are O( TN 2 ). Circuit      26

27   compilation, cell verification, selector queries, feasible repair, numerical enclosure,   27

28   coefficient conversion and serialization are separately counted or bounded in the         28

29   stated implementation. Arbitrary precision multiplies arithmetic counts by the            29

30   cost of the chosen bit operations and requires enough guard precision to meet the         30

31   query error. These are upper bounds for the specified routines, not lower bounds          31

32   against alternative algorithms. In general dimension the original state and action        32
     32

1    covering factors remain visible. No computational saving follows from relabeling                1

2    the identical envelope as a neural circuit.                                                     2

5                              14.1. Identity and deterministic support                              5

6                                                                                                    6
       For a fixed policy m and its own fitted continuation, let
7                                                                                                    7

8                Zm = f 0m ( X0 ) + BT ( g( XT ) − f Tm ( XT ))                                      8

                        + ∑ Bt {ct ( Xt , Am              m                       m

11                                                                                                   11
     Conditional expectation and telescoping show EZm = Jm for either a fixed or ran-
12                                                                                                   12
     dom initial state. Policies in a pair may be driven by common shocks; preservation
13                                                                                                   13
     of each marginal law is sufficient. The innovation dependence between the two
14                                                                                                   14
     policies changes variance, not this identity.
15                                                                                                   15
       For f tm − Tt f tm+1 ≤ um
                               t and Tt
                                        πm m
                                          f t+1 − f tm ≤ dm
                                                          t , the realized conditional residual
16                                                                                                   16

              t , dt ]. The lower bound follows because a feasible action cannot be below
17                                                                                                   17
     the Bellman infimum. Adding the ranges of these residuals, the terminal differences,
18                                                                                                   18
     and the initial difference gives the deterministic support in the article. For a common
19                                                                                                   19
     random initial state the initial difference is piecewise affine on the union of the
20                                                                                                   20
     two knot sets; extrema occur at those knots. For fixed initial states it is evaluated
21                                                                                                   21

22                                                                                                   22

23                                                                                                   23
       The frozen scalar terminal allowance is reconstructed from its stored width. If
24                                                                                                   24
     terminal node error is eT , a cone continuation gives g − f T ∈ [−eT − 2L g h T , eT ], and
25                                                                                                   25
     a spline gives g − f T ∈ [−eT − L g h T , eT + L g h T ]. All signs are retained when forming
26                                                                                                   26
     pair support. Subtracting separate regret bounds would not give this estimand.
27                                                                                                   27

28                                                                                                   28
                         14.2. Bins, discontinuities, and endpoint statistics
29                                                                                                   29

30     Each independent uniform integer specifies one of 240 equal innovation bins,                  30

31   and, for the uniform initial-state law, one such initial bin. The lower and upper               31

32   path computation encloses every continuous point of that bin. Conditional uniform               32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                               33

1    randomization within each independent bin recovers the original continuous uni- 1
2    form law. It need not be explicitly generated because every such value is already              2

3    enclosed.                                                                                      3

4      At a selector boundary, stored rational cuts are enclosed outward. Binary search             4

5    obtains the first and last cells intersecting the state interval. The action interval is the   5

6    hull of all actions in those cells, not merely its two endpoint actions. Propagating           6

7    the hull may be conservative but is valid even for a nonmonotone actor. No path is             7

8    removed. Intersections with the proved invariant state domain and deterministic                8

9    score support preserve enclosure. The stored ambiguity counts quantify this part of            9

10   the computation. These endpoint observations are independent across paths under                10

11   the stated sampling model, even though endpoints and contrasts within a path are               11

12   dependent.                                                                                     12

13     Here are the numerical details of the empirical Bernstein calculation. For a                 13

14   binary64 endpoint sample x1 , . . . , xn ∈ [ a, b], let M = max(| a|, |b|) and γ = (n + 14
15   4)u/(1 − (n + 4)u). Denote the computed mean and mean square by µ
                                                                     e, qe. The exact               15

16   moments obey                                                                                   16

17                                                                                                  17

18                    µ ∈ [µ
                           e − γM, µ
                                   e + γM],          q ∈ [qe − γM2 , qe + γM2 ].                    18

19                                                                                                  19

20
     The bound allows sequential summation, multiplication and division and is conser- 20
21                                                                                                  21
     vative for the implementation’s pairwise sums. Rational arithmetic constructs these
22                                                                                                  22
     intervals from the exact binary64 outputs. There is no uncharged empirical-variance
23                                                                                                  23
     rounding. If [µ− , µ+ ] is the mean enclosure, then
24                                                                                                  24
                                                                
25                                       n                                                          25
                                 V+ =       q+ − min v2
26                                      n−1     v∈[µ− ,µ+ ] +                                       26

27                                                                                                  27

28   is an upper bound for the unbiased sample variance. The minimum is zero if the                 28

29   mean interval contains zero and otherwise the smaller squared endpoint.                        29

30     There are K = 120 contrasts, each with two one-sided tails, and total error α =              30

31   0.01. The bound of Maurer and Pontil (2009) uses log(4K/α) = log(48000) < 11. 31
32   The inequality is verified by the exact positive sum ∑40    j
                                                           j=0 11 /j! > 48000. A valid              32
     34

1    endpoint radius is                                                                      1

2                                        p                 7(b − a) 11                       2
                            R (V + ) =       2V + 11/n +               .
3                                                           3( n − 1)                        3

4    The code rounds the square-root result upward until its exact rational square is at     4

5    least the radicand. All remaining additions are rational. Subtract this radius from     5

6    the lower endpoint’s mean lower bound and add it to the upper endpoint’s mean           6

7    upper bound. Intersect with known support, then round the final reported display        7

8    outward. This proves simultaneous coverage with probability at least 0.99 condi- 8
9    tional on the fixed-policy, independent-bin model. A seed supplies reproducibility,     9

10   not evidence that a pseudorandom generator satisfies a mathematical independence        10

11   axiom.                                                                                  11

12                                                                                           12

13                           14.3. Decision margins and selection                            13

14
       The protocol fixed the superiority threshold at zero and the separate deci- 14
15                                                                                           15
     sion tolerance at 1/1024 before the new comparison. Thus a full interval inside
16                                                                                           16
     [−1/1024, 1/1024] establishes two-sided cost proximity in the normalized units
17                                                                                           17
     on the joint coverage event. It does not establish equality, a signed ranking, or a
18                                                                                           18
     calibrated welfare equivalence. The theorem for choosing among a finite catalogue
19
     is a direct inequality on this same event: Jmb − Jb ≤ Umb and Um ≤ Jm − Jb + Um − Lm . 19
20                                                                                           20
     Minimizing Um + k m gives the stated oracle inequality. Charges are known inputs to
21                                                                                           21
     that decision problem; converting observed seconds to an economic charge requires
22                                                                                           22
     an additional model and is not performed here.
23                                                                                           23
       The complete cost intervals follow. The same fixed policy may appear under
24                                                                                           24
     distinct target labels; its identity is retained in every row. A timing repetition is
25                                                                                           25
     not a new trained policy. All lower and upper endpoint moments, supports, seeds,
26                                                                                           26
     stream hashes, ambiguity counts and input checkpoint hashes are deposited with
27                                                                                           27
     the result records.
28                                                                                           28

29                                                                                           29

30                                                                                           30

7      W is the witness, C the same-critic nearest actor, and S the spline. Every displayed    7

8    endpoint is rounded outward to seven decimal places; the exact rational intervals         8

9    control all decisions and are deposited. All 120 intervals lie inside the predeclared     9

13
                        15. COMPLETE FRONTIERS AND EXPERIMENT RECORDS                          13

14     For each method, the first-crossing function is constant between adjacent values        14

15   of its recorded certified bounds. Partitioning the positive tolerance axis by the union   15

16   of both methods’ bounds therefore gives the exact comparison everywhere, with left        16

17   endpoints included because attainment is weak inequality. The full-prefix Bellman         17

18   count charges every earlier failed rung. Above the largest breakpoint both methods        18

19   use their first rung. An interval in which only one method attains is distinguished       19

20   from an interval in which both attain with different query counts. This descriptive       20

21   reconstruction is not a new experimental target design.                                   21

           16. CONTINUOUS TENSOR COMPILATION AND FEASIBLE WITNESS IDENTITY
28                                                                                            28

29     This section proves Theorem 14 of the article. We state its identities explicitly      29

30   so that the numerical compiler can be checked without relying on an unproved             30

31   interpolation rule. The original labels and original witness indices, not transformed- 31
32   node actions, are the inputs.                                                            32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                 43

1                            16.1. The line transform and its tie invariant                             1

2                                                                                                       2
       For ordered line locations s0 < · · · < sm , attach to each location a pair (q j , a j ),
3
     where q j is a real cost and a j an original index. Order such pairs lexicographically. 3
4                                                                                                       4
     Addition of a scalar to a pair adds it only to the cost. Start with u j = (q j , a j ) and
5                                                                                                       5
     perform
6                                                                                                       6

7               u j ← min{u j , u j−1 + L(s j − s j−1 )},                 j = 1, . . . , m,       (1)   7
                       lex
8                                                                                                       8
                u j ← min{u j , u j+1 + L(s j+1 − s j )},                 j = m − 1, . . . , 0.
9                      lex                                                                              9

10   After the forward pass, induction gives u j = mink≤ j,lex (qk + L(s j − sk ), ak ). The            10

11   backward pass makes u j the minimum over all original line inputs of (qk + L|s j −                 11

12   sk |, ak ). To see this directly, a site to the right can be reached by the monotone               12

13   backward path; a site to the left has already been considered in the forward pass. 13
14   Any path that reverses direction has weakly larger distance because L ≥ 0, so it                   14

15   cannot improve the cost. With L = 0 the original smallest index propagates in                      15

16   both directions. Lexicographic addition and minimum preserve the tie information                   16

17   throughout.                                                                                        17

18     For a tensor grid, define the coordinate operator Tj by taking this minimum along                18

19   coordinate j with all other coordinates fixed. Expanding the finite minima shows                   19

20                                                                                                      20
                             Td · · · T1 (yi , i )(v) = min(yi + L∥v − xi ∥1 , i ).
21                                                   i,lex                                              21

22                                                                                                      22
     Thus the final result is (zv , ov ) in (48). Each grid edge is relaxed at most twice in
23                                                                                                      23
     each coordinate. The edge count is at most dS, and all updates can be made in place
24                                                                                                      24
     with one cost and one index per node. The separable distance-transform part is
25                                                                                                      25
     the classical algorithm; see Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher (2012). Original action
26                                                                                                      26
     owners are auxiliary information propagated by the same minimum operations,
27                                                                                                      27
     not independently recomputed choices.
28                                                                                                      28

29                       16.2. Off-grid extension and the original tie rule                             29

30                                                                                                      30
       For any corner v and any original site i, the triangle inequality gives
31                                                                                                      31

32                      y i + L ∥ x − x i ∥1 ≤ y i + L ∥ v − x i ∥1 + L ∥ x − v ∥1 .                    32
     44

1    Minimizing first over i proves the lower inequality in (49). For the converse, fix             1

2    a site attaining f ( x ). If its jth coordinate is at or to the left of the cell, choose the   2

3    left corner coordinate; if it is at or to the right, choose the right corner coordinate. 3
4    A grid site cannot lie strictly between adjacent grid coordinates. When x lies on              4

5    the cell boundary, either admissible corner choice gives the same needed identity. 5
6    Summing the coordinate identities gives                                                        6

7                                                                                                   7
                                ∥ x − x i ∥1 = ∥ x − v ∥1 + ∥ v − x i ∥1 .
8                                                                                                   8

9                                                                                                   9
     Using this site in zv proves the upper inequality and hence equality.
10                                                                                                  10
       Now take the smallest attaining original index i∗ ( x ). For its associated corner v,
11                                                                                                  11
     equality in the preceding chain implies zv = yi∗ + L∥v − xi∗ ∥1 . If the original owner
12                                                                                                  12
     ov were smaller than i∗ , then
13                                                                                                  13

14                        y o v + L ∥ x − x o v ∥1 ≤ z v + L ∥ x − v ∥1 = f ( x ).                  14

15                                                                                                  15
     It would be an attaining original site with a smaller index, a contradiction. Therefore
16                                                                                                  16
     ov = i∗ ( x ). Evaluating the original cones of all corner owners and taking their lexi-
17                                                                                                  17
     cographic minimum proves (50). This argument covers inconsistent or dominated
18                                                                                                  18
     labels: it never uses zv = yv .
19                                                                                                  19
       Attaching the original action aov to a transformed label is essential. Attaching av
20                                                                                                  20
     need not preserve the policy. Under the established identity, both implementations
21                                                                                                  21
     select i∗ ( x ) and apply the same feasible repair Γi∗ ( x ). They consequently induce
22                                                                                                  22
     the same action at every state, the same controlled transition law and, by backward
23                                                                                                  23
     evaluation, the same policy value. The argument is not restricted to expected
24                                                                                                  24
     costs; identity of the actual policy preserves any well-defined common recursive
25                                                                                                  25
     evaluation. It does not equate two policies generated from different rounded label
26                                                                                                  26
     arrays.
27                                                                                                  27

28                                                                                                  28
                           16.3. Finite arithmetic and uncertain queries
29                                                                                                  29

30     All preprocessing inputs in the executable study are exact rational representations          30

31   of the stored numerical labels, slopes and coordinate knots. Comparisons in (1) are            31

32   exact. The compiler stores the resulting rational zv and integer ov . Its arithmetic- 32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                             45

1    operation count does not suppress numerator/denominator growth: coefficient bit              1

2    lengths and actual preprocessing time are recorded.                                          2

3      At an exact represented point, outward arithmetic encloses each term of (49);              3

4    taking the minimum lower endpoint and the minimum upper endpoint encloses                    4

5    the true minimum. At an uncertain point x ∈ D, a midpoint value plus and minus               5

6    L supx∈ D ∥ x − xb∥1 is also a valid value enclosure. This is the continuation’s Lipschitz   6

7    property, not an actor regularity assertion.                                                 7

8      For the actor, let C( D ) be the finitely many tensor cells whose closures meet a          8

9    query box. The previous proof implies that every exact winning original index at             9

10   any point in that box belongs to {ov : v ∈ V (C ), C ∈ C( D )}. An interval lower bound      10

11   above the smallest available upper bound rules out a candidate; all other candidates         11

12   are retained. Their action hull includes the exact actor at every permitted state. 12
13   Repair, downward action quantization, costs and transitions are then evaluated by            13

14   interval extensions. This may enlarge the path enclosure, but cannot improve the             14

15   statistical comparison by hiding a discontinuity. A degenerate ambiguous box is              15

16   resolved by exact original-cone comparison with the same smallest-index tie rule.            16

17     The off-grid work is bounded by the corner count only for a point or one-cell              17

18   query. A large uncertainty box can intersect many cells and requires their union. We         18

19   do not use the point-query complexity theorem to claim dimension-independent or              19

20   uncertainty-independent simulation work. The actual ambiguity and actor-query                20

21   counts are included in the direct-comparison records.                                        21

22                                                                                                22

23                                                                                                23

24                                                                                                24
                          16.4. Work to implemented economic accuracy
25                                                                                                25

26     For Corollary 15, the deterministic primitive account gives G ≤ CT,p /N + 2ST ξ + 26
27   2β T ξ T . Under its allocations the first term is at most ε/4 and the two numerical terms   27

28   sum to at most ε/4. The exact-selector acquisition addition is A2−b + K∆ ≤ ε/2, so           28

29   the implemented loss is at most ε. A sufficient integer bit count can be found by            29

30   increasing b until the rational inequality holds; no unchecked logarithm is required. 30
31   If observation has a fee, the augmented objective also pays λdbST . One cannot send          31

32   b to infinity at fixed positive price and infer a vanishing augmented loss.                  32
     46

1      There are ( N + 1)2 state nodes, N + 1 actions at each node and N innovation                    1

2    midpoints. With a compiled continuation each midpoint requires a fixed number of                  2

3    two-dimensional primitive and corner operations, while transforming each date’s                   3

4    labels and storing its feasible actors requires O( N 2 ) further algebraic work. Thus the         4

5    full backward construction has O( TN 4 ) arithmetic operations. The dense alternative             5

6    multiplies the midpoint workload by a further ( N + 1)2 . The finite-bit actor applies            6

7    the same compiled owner rule and a constant-dimensional capacity repair. For a                    7

8    dyadic ladder ending at N, ∑ j≥0 ( N/2 j )q ≤ N q /(1 − 2−q ) for q > 0; this charges all         8

9    previous construction attempts. The storage of the entire ladder of checkpoints also              9

10   has the same O( TN 2 ) scalar order.                                                              10

11     These are sufficient algebraic upper bounds, not lower bounds for conventional                  11

12   methods. A p-bit elementary operation is charged at its actual or separately bounded              12

13   arithmetic cost, and exact rational owners carry their coefficient sizes. Statistical             13

14   policy comparison is outside this construction bound and is charged as its own                    14

15   joint service. No physical fsync latency, unknown optimizer iteration count, or                   15

16   unrestricted uncertainty-box enumeration is hidden inside the arithmetic order.                   16

17                                                                                                     17

18    17. NONUNIFORM FITTED-VALUE COMPARISON AND MULTIDIMENSIONAL PRIMITIVES                           18

                  17.1. A common all-state criterion on unequal coordinate grids

22   mization returns yi with −et ≤ yi − gt ( xi ) ≤ DtQ k t + et and original action ai with          22

23   Qt ( f t+1 ; xi , ai ) ≤ yi + et . For multilinear interpolation on a rectangle of side lengths   23

24   hCj , its nonnegative barycentric weights have sum one and satisfy                                24

25                                                                                                     25

26                                               2( x j − x −      +
                                                            j )( x j − x j )       1                   26

                  ∑ wi (x)∥x − xi ∥1 = ∑                    hCj

                                                                                                       27
                   i                         j
28                                                                                                     28

29   where ht is half the sum of the largest coordinate gaps. Hence the optimal-residual               29

30   upper bound is DtQ k t + et + Lt ht .                                                             30

31     Deploy the original action at the nearest coordinatewise node, choosing the                     31

32   smaller original index on a tie, with the same state-feasible repair. On its closed               32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                   47

1    nearest-node cell Di , feasible objective transport gives                                           1

2                                                                                                        2

3
                   Qt ( f t+1 ; x, Γi ( x )) − f t ( x ) ≤ et + yi + Lt ∥ x − xi ∥1 − f t ( x ).         3

4                                                                                                        4
     Intersect Di with tensor cells and split at xi in every coordinate. On each resulting
5                                                                                                        5
     rectangle, the expression after et is multi-affine: the absolute value has fixed sign and
6                                                                                                        6
     f t is multilinear. A multi-affine function attains a maximum at a rectangle vertex,
7                                                                                                        7
     as repeated one-dimensional maximization shows. It therefore suffices to test the
8                                                                                                        8
     left midpoint, node, and right midpoint in each coordinate, at most 3d vertices per
9                                                                                                        9
     node, using outward arithmetic. Denote this computed upper bound by Et . The
10                                                                                                       10
     common one-sided policy criterion becomes
11                                                                                                       11


                   GF = ∑ Bt ( DtQ k t + 2et + Lt ht + Et ) + BT (2eT + 2L g h T ).

14                                                                                                       14
     The witness criterion remains (41) with exact repair. Both compare against the
15                                                                                                       15
     original optimum over all admissible adapted policies. Neither treats a sampled
16                                                                                                       16
     residual as an all-state error.
17                                                                                                       17
       The interpolant’s ℓ1 Lipschitz constant is the largest absolute edge slope: each
18                                                                                                       18
     partial derivative is a convex combination of edge slopes, and the dual norm of ℓ1
19                                                                                                       19
     is ℓ∞ . Slopes are computed exactly from rational knots and labels. This modulus,
20                                                                                                       20
     not a neural modulus borrowed from another generator, is passed to that method’s
21                                                                                                       21
     previous-date target construction.

23                                                                                                       23
                         17.2. The prespecified error-driven coordinate grid
24                                                                                                       24

25     The adaptive conventional comparator is a nonuniform tensor FVI method, not                       25

26   a sparse grid or locally adaptive quadtree. At every date and resolution N > 4, it                  26

27   computes a fresh 5d pilot array using its own future continuation and the same N                    27

28   feasible action fractions and N innovation midpoints. At terminal date it evaluates                 28

29   the terminal primitive instead. These evaluations are charged even when a later                     29

30   grid repeats a pilot node.                                                                          30

31     For coordinate j, let D j,k , k = 1, 2, 3, be the maximum, across all other pilot coor- 31
32   dinates, of the absolute second difference |yk−1 − 2yk + yk+1 |. Give coarse interval               32
     48

1    [k/4, (k + 1)/4], k = 0, . . . , 3, weight                                                          1

2                                                                                                        2

3
                            w j,k = 1 + 64 max{ D j,max(1,k) , D j,min(3,k+1) }.                         3

4                                                                                                        4
     Start with the four coarse intervals. Repeatedly bisect the interval maximizing w j,k
5                                                                                                        5
     times its squared length, breaking ties at the leftmost interval, until there are N
6                                                                                                        6
     intervals on that axis. All rankings and knots are rational. This positive curvature
7                                                                                                        7
     heuristic and its factor 64 were fixed in the scientific source before the full catalogue;
8                                                                                                        8
     no reported outcome selects them. The final cover uses the actual largest gaps. In
9                                                                                                        9
     particular, preferentially refining one area can worsen a global covering allowance
10                                                                                                       10
     elsewhere. The common certificate exposes that tradeoff rather than crediting the
11                                                                                                       11
     adaptive method with an assumed uniform radius.
12                                                                                                       12

28                                                                                             28
                     18. OBSERVATION CHARGES AND DIRECT COST INTERVALS
29                                                                                             29

30
                             18.1. The sensor account and integer choice                       30

31     Each observed coordinate cell has width 2−b and midpoint error at most 2−(b+1) , 31
32   including the boundary cells. For the affine capacity, evaluating the lower endpoint      32
     50

1    of the observation box produces a feasible lower capacity. Its distance from the          1

2    true capacity is at most 2K A r (b). Downward action rounding by ∆ adds at most           2

3    ∆ to repair displacement. Therefore the acquisition addition at date t is 2Lt r (b) + 3
4    DtQ (2KtA r (b) + ∆t ). Summing discounted additions and adding the deterministic         4

5    observation charge gives (59). The same summation does not claim that a reference         5

6    policy-loss bound is a policy-cost point estimate.                                        6

7      The integer precision comparisons follow from the successive difference C −             7

8    A2−(b+1) . If this difference vanishes, both b and b + 1 minimize locally, and mono- 8
9    tonicity makes them global minimizers; the smaller is recorded. Every candidate           9

10   precision, charge, allowance and augmented bound is stored as a rational num- 10
11   ber. Checking the two neighbors, or all candidates in the finite range, is an exact       11

12   optimization certificate for this upper-bound objective.                                  12

13                                                                                             13

14                                                                                             14
                             18.2. Continuous-law path enclosures
15                                                                                             15

16     For a uniform initial law, two independent forty-bit bins cover the initial co- 16
17   ordinates. For a fixed initial vector, the coordinate inputs are exact. At each date      17

18   a further independent bin encloses the common continuous innovation. The bin              18

19   width is 2−40 for an initial coordinate and 2−44 for the shock interval of total length   19

20   1/16.                                                                                     20

21     For each controller separately, its state box determines all possible observation       21

22   bins; exact midpoints of those bins form an observation enclosure. The exact actor        22

23   at every possible observation is enclosed using original witness owners or the            23

24   nearest-node FVI cells. Capacity repair uses the lowest capacity on each possible         24

25   observation box. Its action hull includes all possible repaired and quantized actions. 25
26   The natural interval extensions of cost and transition then propagate the enclosure       26

27   to the next date. The compiler may accelerate these evaluations but never replaces a      27

28   path box by an unchecked midpoint actor. Dependence between the two policies is           28

29   arbitrary inside a paired path; their marginals retain the prescribed innovation law. 29
30     The running-cost support follows from the separate bounds ∑ j ( x j − 5/8)2 ≤           30

31   25/32, ( x1 − x2 )2 /4 ≤ 1/4, 2(1/2 − x1 − x2 )2+ ≤ 1/2, pa2 ≤ p/16, and 4a4 ≤ 1/64. 31
32   Terminal cost is at most 37/16. These inequalities prove (62); separate maxima need       32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                            51

1    not occur at the same state. The wider support is retained as a conservative input,           1

2    rather than estimated from the simulated sample.                                              2

3                                                                                                  3

4                               18.3. Moment and probability accounts                              4

5                                                                                                  5
          For a bounded endpoint array X1 , . . . , Xn ∈ [ a, b], write M = max(| a|, |b|), u =
6                                                                                                  6
     2−53 and γ = (n + 4)u/(1 − (n + 4)u). The implementation encloses the computed
7                                                                                                  7
     mean by adding and subtracting γM; it enlarges the computed mean square by
8                                                                                                  8
     γM2 . These conservative bounds cover multiplication, summation and division in
9                                                                                                  9
     the finite arrays used here, with no overflow. If the mean enclosure is [m− , m+ ] and
10                                                                                                 10
     the second-moment upper bound is q+ , an unbiased sample-variance upper bound
11                                                                                                 11
     is
12                                                                                                 12
                                                                             
                                          n
13                          s2+ = max 0,             q+ −      min        m2        .              13
                                         n−1                m∈[m− ,m+ ]
14                                                                                                 14

15   The minimum square is zero exactly when the interval contains zero. All moment                15

16   bounds are converted to rationals before computing the confidence allowance. A                16

17   square-root upper endpoint is checked by exact squaring and rounded upward until              17

18   valid. The rational partial sum ∑40    j
                                      j=0 10 /j! > 19200 = 4K/α verifies the logarithmic           18

19   allowance ℓ = 10.                                                                             19

20        The empirical-Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009), applied to the           20

21   two bounded endpoint samples, gives (63)–(64). Each of the 2K one-sided asser- 21
22   tions receives failure probability α/(2K ). The union bound requires no indepen- 22
23   dence across policy-pair tasks, but each task’s endpoint sample uses the stipulated           23

24   independent-bin model. Stored PCG64 seeds and stream hashes give deterministic                24

25   reproducibility only. The path enclosures are deterministic numerical statements;             25

26   the confidence level is a separate sampling statement under that model.                       26

27        For an installed FVI controller, changing to witness costs k + Dµ,b . For an installed   27

28   witness controller, changing to FVI costs k − Dµ,b . The sign tests and no-recoupment         28

29   decision in Theorem 20 follow. They concern the chosen transaction fee and initial            29

30   law. They neither establish welfare equivalence under an unmodelled calibration               30

31   nor prove that both controllers are close to the optimum merely because they are              31

32   close to each other.                                                                          32

1                        19. FULL WORK RECORDS AND PRESERVATION                              1

2                                                                                            2

3      For each completed service, let gr be its exact bound at rung r and wr its accu- 3
4    mulated clock through that rung’s durable checkpoint. The first-crossing work at        4

5    tolerance ε is wmin{r:gr ≤ε} , with no attainment when this set is empty. Sorting the   5

6    union of all exact bound breakpoints yields a complete partition of the positive        6

7    tolerance axis into left-closed, right-open intervals on which every method’s first     7

8    crossing is fixed. Bounds need not be monotone for this construction: the earliest      8

9    qualifying rung is always used. The lowest interval with no attainment and the          9

10   unbounded upper interval are retained.                                                  10

11     Every interval in the machine-readable frontier contains each method’s selected       11

12   resolution, median/minimum/maximum prefix wall time, CPU time, component                12

13   counts and durable checkpoint size. The three repetitions are deterministic con- 13
14   structions with separate process clocks; they are not independent economic tasks        14

15   or neural initializations. The dimension stress cells have one execution and are        15

16   labelled accordingly. Peak resident memory includes the interpreter and warm-up. 16
17   The internal prefix starts at primitives after the fixed non-economic warm-up; the      17

18   parent process records the startup-inclusive duration separately. CPU affinity and      18

19   numerical-library threads are fixed, while CPU frequency is not controlled.             19

20     The service includes target generation, adaptive pilots, exact compilation,           20

21   continuous-law integration and its remainder, actor extraction, certification, failed   21

22   rungs, serialization and a fixed finite acquired-deployment checksum. That check- 22
23   sum is a measured deployment component, not an empirical substitute for the             23

24   uniform acquisition theorem. An independent direct policy-cost experiment is a          24

25   separate joint service. Its clock includes both frozen-policy loads and compilation,    25

26   hash checks, common paths, repair, statistics and durable output. This pair-specific    26

27   cost is reported separately; it is not assigned silently to one controller or called    27

28   zero. Historical R46 scalar prefix-time curves are reconstructed from their original    28

29   records without retiming or adding them to R48 clocks.                                  29

30     The scalar, original constrained and precision findings remain historical evi- 30
31   dence. The new compiled construction is a separate prospective service, while the       31

32   fixed-object compiler diagnostic retains the old labels and original witnesses. This    32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                        53

1    separation prevents faster evaluation of a frozen object from being misreported as                        1

2    better optimizer reliability or a newly superior policy.                                                  2

3                                                                                                              3
                          20. EVERY DIRECT CONSTRAINED-POLICY INTERVAL
4                                                                                                              4

5      Intervals concern witness minus FVI expected resource cost. The fee column                              5

6    records the two-direction no-recoupment decision at k = 1/64. The familywise                              6

7    statement is conditional on the independent-bin model. Displayed endpoints are                            7

8    rounded outward.                                                                                          8

29                                                                                                               29
      Counts are tolerance intervals, not probabilities or weighted economic tasks. W is the R46 witness
30    and S its spline comparator. These are unaltered historical internal prefix clocks, not R48 timings;       30

31    they are never added to new compiler or direct-comparison clocks. The exact partitions, selected           31

32    checkpoints and all three repeated times are retained.                                                     32

1                                 CURRENT R52 DEVELOPMENT                                     1

3                         22. PRIMITIVE DOMAIN AND EXACT MODULI                               3

4                                                                                             4

5      Use the family in main equations (112)–(113), with d = 2, 3, 4. Capacity is between    5

6    1/8 and 1/4 and has ℓ1 modulus 1/(8d). Every component of the dynamics is at             6

7    least 1/32. A direct upper bound is 29/32 for the larger action exposure and 27/32       7

8    for the smaller one. Thus the cube is invariant without an action-dependent exit         8

9    event. The numerical clipping to [0, 1] is inactive on the true primitive image; it      9

10   does not authorize out-of-domain evaluation.                                             10

11     For d ≥ 2, the sums of the absolute entries in a state column of the transition        11

12   derivative are bounded by 1/2 + 1/16 + 1/8 = 11/16. The action derivative has ℓ1         12

13   norm ∑ j γ j . The action derivative of current cost is 2pa + 16a3 ≤ p/2 + 1/4. These    13

14   are bounds between feasible pairs, not a claim that the same action is feasible at       14

15   both states.                                                                             15

16     On either shortage region the state gradient is affine. The derivative of the          16

17   squared-target and imbalance terms in coordinate j, after multiplication by d, is        17

18   4( x j − 5/8) + (2x j − x j−1 − x j+1 )/2. In the shortage region add −8(1/2 − 2 ∑ x/d). 18
19   The terminal target derivative replaces 4 by 8. A linear functional on each region       19

20   reaches its extrema at region vertices. For the declared dimensions, these are cube      20

21   vertices and vertices with all but at most one coordinate at zero or one and ∑ x = d/4. 21
22   Exact evaluation gives absolute derivative bounds 13/(2d) and 9/d. The added             22

23   regression verifies these vertices rationally. The proof does not assert that these      23

24   convenient formulas remain valid for untested arbitrary d.                               24

25     The state covering radius of a nonuniform tensor grid is half the sum of its maxi- 25
26   mum coordinate spacings. Each action net is generated from a downward enclosure          26

27   of capacity. Its coverage is at most 1/(8K ) + 2−48 under the implemented binary64       27

28   construction. This last conservative allowance covers capacity enclosure, multiplica- 28
29   tion, division and the final downward displacement on the bounded action domain. 29
30   Fixed spacing and valid interval arithmetic are checked; a negative action is clipped    30

31   to zero. Downward quantization on deployment preserves feasibility because the           31

32   capacity itself is enclosed from below over the complete acquisition cell.               32
     64

1                    23. NUMERICAL LABELS AND THE SEPARATED ACCOUNT                                1

2
       An objective query has lower and upper endpoints for its midpoint-sum expec- 2
3                                                                                                  3
     tation, plus the deterministic continuous-law integration remainder. Labels are
4                                                                                                  4
     rounded on the inherited dyadic lattice, and the largest distance to the objective
5                                                                                                  5
     endpoints supplies the query allowance et . Selecting the least rounded label and
6                                                                                                  6
     retaining its original action costs one allowance in the label comparison and another
7                                                                                                  7
     in the complete selected-policy account. It does not require a second copy of the
8                                                                                                  8
     action-cover term in main equation (71).
9                                                                                                  9
       For a uniform scalar bin of width w, the mean absolute distance to its midpoint
10                                                                                                 10
     is w/4. Here w = 1/(16M) and the transition’s shock modulus is d. Therefore the
11                                                                                                 11
     numerical account decomposes into βdLt+1 /(64M) and the remaining arithmetic
12                                                                                                 12
     width. The recorded primitive coefficients in main equation (99) reconstruct each
13
     witness bound as a/N + b/K + q/M + η with a nonnegative numerical remainder. 13
14                                                                                                 14
     The release checks this decomposition at all witness rungs, as well as the full signed
15                                                                                                 15
     formula for every conventional rung.
16                                                                                                 16
       The continuous allocation in main Proposition 38 minimizes a leading product,
17                                                                                                 17
     not a full finite-precision program. With positive coefficient ci and exponent pi ,
18
     changing variables to error contribution xi = ci /ri gives the objective ∑i pi log xi . Its   18

19                                                                                                 19
     constrained first-order condition is xi = min( x̄i , pi λ). The multiplier exists uniquely
20                                                                                                 20
     until every cap binds. A zero coefficient has no benefit from refinement, so its
21                                                                                                 21
     coordinate stays at the resource lower bound. Upward dyadic rounding keeps every
22                                                                                                 22
     error term weakly smaller. Compiler setup, integer counts, coefficient bit lengths,
23                                                                                                 23
     adaptive pilots, memory and durable output are not removed by that relaxation.
24                                                                                                 24

                     24. WHY THE COMPILED ACTOR IS THE ORIGINAL ACTOR                              25

26     At a tensor node the distance transform minimizes over original sites, not merely           26

27   neighboring transformed nodes. Each forward relaxation along a coordinate line                27

28   compares the current value–owner pair with the preceding pair plus the weighted               28

29   coordinate distance; the backward pass does the same from the opposite direction. 29
30   The invariant is the lexicographic minimum over all processed original sites in the           30

31   processed coordinates. Separability proves the invariant after the final coordinate. 31
32   Keeping only the winning value would lose the actor identity.                                 32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                               65

1      For an off-grid point, choose a cell corner coordinatewise between the point and             1

2    its lexicographically first minimizing site. Distances add exactly. At that corner an          2

3    owner giving a smaller value, or a tied value and smaller original index, contradicts          3

4    the minimizer at the original point. This proves the owner identity even for incon- 4
5    sistent labels, zero slope, boundary cells and ties. Nonuniform coordinate spacings            5

6    enter only the exact distances.                                                                6

7      In interval execution, value expressions are enclosed outward. Actor candidates              7

8    whose lower objective exceeds another candidate’s upper objective can be discarded;            8

9    the remaining possibilities must be retained. An exact point tie is resolved by                9

10   rational comparison and the original index rule. A genuine uncertain state box is              10

11   not an exact point: the hull of its surviving actions is propagated. Boxes crossing            11

12   cells use the union of all relevant corners. These operations may cost more than one           12

13   point query, and the direct records retain actor and measurement ambiguity counts. 13
14                                                                                                  14

15                25. ACQUISITION FOR WITNESS AND CONVENTIONAL POLICIES                             15

16
       Let x ∈ C be the true state and xb the reported midpoint, with radius r in ℓ1 . Fix          16

17                                                                                                  17
     the selected original node xi and node action ai . The ideal repair at the true state
18
     is feasible and within K A ∥ x − xi ∥1 of ai . Replacing that repair by the cell-wide          18

19
     lower-capacity repair followed by downward spacing ∆ changes the action by at                  19

20
     most 2K A r + ∆. Thus                                                                          20

21                                                                                                  21

22                    Q( x, aimpl ) ≤ yi + e + Lgr ∥ x − xi ∥1 + D (2K A r + ∆).                    22

23                                                                                                  23

24   For a witness selection at xb, yi + Lgr ∥ xb − xi ∥1 = f ( xb), up to a separately certified   24

25   selector error. The triangle inequality and L f = Lgr yield the allowance in main              25

26   equation (73).                                                                                 26

27     For conventional nearest-node selection, subtract and add f ( xb). The expres- 27
28   sion yi + Lgr ∥ xb − xi ∥1 − f ( xb) is bounded by its complete nearest-cell maximum           28

29   Et . The remaining terms are at most ( Lgr + L f + 2DK A )r + D∆. This proves main             29

30   equation (109) without a continuity assumption on the actor. It also explains why              30

31   applying the witness coefficient unchanged to an interpolated conventional critic              31

32   would be incorrect when the actual critic modulus differs.                                     32
     66

1      For two-state records, r (b) = 2−b and the common spacing is 1/4096. The all-state    1

2    bit criterion is therefore G + K∆ + A2−b + 2λb ∑t<T βt , where A uses the appropriate   2

3    actual critic modulus and K∆ = ∑t βt Dt /4096. The interval-net criterion uses actual   3

                  26. CONTINUOUS PATH LAW AND SIMULTANEOUS INFERENCE
7                                                                                            7

8      Conditional on all construction records, the studied controllers are fixed. Each      8

9    simulation row draws independent uniform indices for the initial coordinates            9

10   and each innovation. The interval simulator encloses the complete continuous            10

11   cell corresponding to those indices; no bin midpoint is substituted for the true        11

12   path distribution in the estimand. Uniformly mixing conditional continuous cells        12

13   reproduces the original uniform initial and innovation laws. Common innovations         13

14   may couple two policies, but each follows its own endogenous trajectory.                14

15     In dimension two, a valid support interval for a raw discounted path-cost differ- 15
16   ence is [− H, H ], where                                                                16
                                      99 + 4p       37
17
                                 H=           ∑
                                         64 t<T
                                                βt + β T .
                                                    16
                                                                                             17

18                                                                                           18

19   Indeed, current cost is nonnegative and at most (99 + 4p)/64 by separately bound- 19
20   ing squared-target, imbalance, shortage and action costs. Terminal cost is at most      20

21   37/16. These conservative bounds apply to every feasible repaired path. Clipping        21

22   interval endpoints to the support preserves inclusion. Lower endpoint expectations      22

23   lie below the true cost difference; upper endpoint expectations lie above it.           23

24     The stored moments consist of outward sample-mean intervals and upper sample- 24
25   variance bounds for each endpoint sample. In (104), the square root is rounded          25

26   upward after checking its square against the rational radicand. The linear remain- 26
27   der is rational. With K ≤ 300, α = 1/100 and ℓ = 13, a finite positive Taylor sum       27

28   verifies e13 > 4K/α. The bounded empirical-Bernstein inequalities of Maurer and         28

29   Pontil (2009) and a union bound therefore yield the claimed simultaneous event. 29
30   Dependence between contrasts through shared economic objects is permitted; in- 30
31   dependence within each bin-index sample is the statistical assumption. The fixed        31

32   pseudorandom seeds do not prove that assumption.                                        32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                             67

1      A first-crossing choice is measurable with respect to construction information. 1
2    The conditional coverage assertion consequently remains valid after this choice. 2
3    A bit selector or a fee queried after seeing the simultaneous intervals also needs          3

4    no additional error allocation: on the one event, each underlying cost difference           4

5    already belongs to its interval. This does not validate new initial laws, new trained       5

6    policies, or unrecorded contrasts. Old and new study families have separate error           6

7    accounts; combining them incurs the corresponding union bound.                              7

10                      27. EXACT FRONTIERS AND DECISION SEMANTICS                               10

11                                                                                               11

12     For a fixed construction ladder, the first-crossing function can change only at a         12

13   recorded certificate. A sorted union of all such values, together with zero, partitions     13

14   all positive tolerances into left-closed, right-open intervals. At an attained endpoint     14

15   the weak inequality in the stopping rule applies. Later nonmonotone or dominated            15

16   certificates need not change the function, but remain in the raw record. Adjacent           16

17   intervals with identical returned rungs may be merged for display; the exact rational       17

18   partition is deposited.                                                                     18

19     For a direct interval [l, u], the gain from replacing witness by FVI is in [l, u];        19

20   the reverse gain is in [−u, −l ]. The fee max(0, u, −l ) suffices to certify that neither   20

21   switch is strictly profitable. The smaller max(0, l, −u) only says that neither switch      21

22   has an interval lying wholly in its strictly profitable region. When zero lies inside       22

23   a wide interval, this latter threshold is zero and is not evidence that a free switch       23

24   cannot pay. The release reports both objects.                                               24

25     For information price λ, each bit choice supplies lower and upper affine net-cost         25

26   lines. Enumerating all their nonnegative intersections partitions the price half-line. 26
27   On an open interval, the active lower and upper lines are fixed. At a boundary all          27

28   lines are compared exactly with smallest-bit ties. The difference between the active        28

29   upper and lower lines is the selected implementation’s valid regret upper bound. 29
30   The identity line at 16 bits is exactly zero before information charges. No simulation      30

31   count or time is assigned to an unperformed identity experiment; its arithmetic             31

32   identity is recorded explicitly.                                                            32

25     At fixed x, the transition is affine in a. The terminal objective is a sum of convex           25

26   quadratics and squared positive parts of affine functions. Its expectation is convex. 26
27   The action cost pa2 + 4a4 has second derivative 2p + 48a2 ≥ 2p. Thus final condi- 27
28   tional cost has a unique continuous minimizer, possibly on the capacity boundary. 28
29   Its lattice minimizer lies at the first lattice point with nonnegative derivative or the         29

30   preceding point, with boundary truncation. The implementation searches at most                   30

31   the fixed 4096 denominator’s range, compares the two costs, and uses exact rational              31

32   comparison when outward point intervals fail to decide a tie. The incumbent need                 32
