                           30. SIMULTANEOUS ACTUAL-COST INFERENCE
16                                                                                                       16

17     The R50 primary family contains 25 pairs of baseline generators. Each group con- 17
18   tains four policies—the two incumbents and their final repairs—and produces four                    18

19   absolute costs and six pairwise differences. Thus there are 250 sampled estimands. 19
20   Their selection uses frozen construction information only. Within each group the                    20

21   continuous initial law and the first T − 1 innovations are represented by forty-bit                 21

22   bins with outward propagation. The last innovation is integrated analytically. Con- 22
23   ditional expectation over this last innovation preserves each policy’s expected total               23

24   cost. It reduces simulation variance without substituting a new innovation law.                     24

25     The path-cost support derived in the retained inference section applies in dimen- 25
26   sions two through four: the target and imbalance terms are coordinate averages                      26

27   with the same bounds, the shortage term is bounded by 1/2, and feasible actions                     27

28   do not exceed 1/4. Consequently 0 ≤ J-score ≤ H, with H as in Section 26; a paired                  28

29   score lies in [− H, H ]. An incumbent-minus-own-repair contrast is nonnegative by                   29

30   the independently proved gate and is bounded by H. Because the earlier trajec- 30
31   tory is common, its numerical enclosure uses the centered final contrast instead of                 31

32   subtracting two complete total-cost intervals.                                                      32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           71

1      For endpoint observations Xi ∈ [ A, B], the retained empirical-Bernstein bound          1

2    uses                                                                                      2
                                q
3                                                                                              3
                         Rn =       2Var( X )ℓ/n + 7( B − A)ℓ/[3(n − 1)],
4                                                                                              4

5    with outward moment intervals, an upper sample variance, and a rationally checked         5

6    upward square root. Lower endpoints and upper endpoints each receive a one- 6
7    sided bound. Set n = 131072, ℓ = 13 and α = 1/100. The family cap is 512; an exact        7

8    finite Taylor sum proves e13 > 4(512)/α. A union bound therefore covers all 250           8

9    actual-cost estimands under the independent-bin model. The cap leaves room but            9

10   does not certify unrecorded models or initial laws. The pseudorandom stream               10

11   and its hash establish reproducibility, not independence of ideal observations. The       11

12   confidence event for the retained R49 family is separate; combining old and new           12

13   statistical assertions incurs the sum of their failure probabilities.                     13

                          31. MECHANISM AND ECONOMIC FRONTIERS
15                                                                                             15

16     Denote witness and FVI by W, F and their final repairs by W + , F + . Pure algebra      16

17   yields                                                                                    17

18                                                                                             18
                      JW − JF = ( JW − JW + ) + ( JW + − JF+ ) − ( JF − JF+ ).           (4)
19                                                                                             19

20   All three terms concern actual costs. The first and last are direct measures of remov- 20
21   able final-action loss; the middle retains differences from earlier actions, acquired     21

22   states, continuation approximation and the gate’s conservatism. It is not identified      22

23   as a single causal contribution of neural representation. The experiment reports each     23

24   term, accepted and blocked cell counts, final action means, final state means and         24

25   earlier action means by date. This exposes how much of the original ordering can          25

26   be removed without retraining the continuation. It does not claim that the residual       26

27   has been exhaustively decomposed into mutually independent mechanisms.                    27

28     For finite intervals [ L j , Uj ], the upper-net-cost choice’s true regret is bounded   28

29   by the selected upper line minus the minimum lower line. To see why this is               29

30   conservative, consider two overlapping cost intervals: their midpoint ordering            30

31   may reverse within the same event. The upper-envelope decision remains valid but          31

32   cannot identify the true minimizer. Pairwise centered intervals can certify individual    32
     72

1    replacements more sharply than the absolute frontier. For any fixed installation fee,                                    1

2    transmission conversion factor and nonnegative resource price, add their declared                                        2

3    costs before applying the comparison. A new price does not require new sampling                                          3

4    on the same finite event. A new sensing policy does.                                                                     4

       P ROOF OF P ROPOSITION 29:         Take T = 1, zero stage cost, β 0 = 1, and two deter-
14                                                                                               14
     ministic actions leading to different terminal states y0 , y1 . Let w > 0 and 0 < δ < w.
15                                                                                               15
     Set
16                                                                                               16

17                  h1 (y0 ) = w,   h1 (y1 ) = δ,      g(y0 ) = 2w,   g(y1 ) = δ.                17

18                                                                                               18
     The exact terminal error lies in [0, w]. The incumbent chooses y0 and the exact
19                                                                                               19
     h1 -greedy proposal chooses y1 . Its exact contrast is d = δ − w, with α = ζ = 0. The
20                                                                                               20
     safe upper comparison is d + w = δ > 0, so the gate retains the incumbent. Its
21                                                                                               21
     actual optimality gap is 2w − δ. Sending δ down to zero establishes sharpness. This
22                                                                                               22
     example also shows why rejecting an uncertified proposal is not evidence that the
23                                                                                               23
     proposal lacks a true economic benefit.                                          Q.E.D.
24                                                                                               24

25     32.0.0.1. Computed verification and logical scope. The source-bound exact-rational        25

26   tests use finite transition matrices, signed residual propagation, and independently        26

27   evaluated policy and optimal costs. They verify policy nonincrease, both gate cases,        27

28   the one-pass recurrence, constant-shift invariance, finite-horizon exact termina- 28
29   tion, and the two-action sharpness construction. They are regression tests for the          29

30   implementation of the displayed formulas; the preceding arguments, rather than              30

31   enumeration, prove the theorems for the stated model class. When bands are statisti- 31
32   cal, all passes and proposed comparisons require a common simultaneous coverage             32
     74

1    event or a valid sequential error allocation. Reusing a confidence interval outside                            1

2    its stated family does not satisfy the hypotheses.                                                             2

3                                                                                                                   3

4
                      33. PROOFS FOR POLICY-RELEVANT CONTINUATION ERRORS                                            4

5         P ROOF OF T HEOREM 30:           Fix a pass and suppress its index. For any feasible a, b,                5

6    subtraction of the two Bellman expressions gives                                                               6

7                                                                                                                   7

8    Qt ( Jtπ+1 ; x, a) − Qt ( Jtπ+1 ; x, b) = Qt (ht+1 ; x, a) − Qt (ht+1 ; x, b) + β t ( Pta − Ptb )et+1 ( x ).   8

9                                                                                                                   9
     Thus the accepted proposal has nonpositive true advantage relative to the incum-
10                                                                                                                  10
     bent. At a rejected state the implemented action is exactly the incumbent, whose
11                                                                     +                                            11
     advantage is zero. Backward induction gives J π ≤ J π . This proves safety even
12                                                                                                                  12
     when only the proposal–incumbent contrast is certified.
13                                                                                                                  13
       For accuracy, suppose first that the proposal is accepted. For every feasible a, its
14                                                                                                                  14
     approximate greediness and the uniform pair certificate imply
15                                                                                                                  15

16                                                                                                                  16
                                Qt ( Jtπ+1 ; x, v) − Qt ( Jtπ+1 ; x, a) ≤ αt + β t χt .
17                                                                                                                  17

18   Taking the infimum over a requires neither existence of a minimizer nor a new                                  18

19   selection assumption. If the proposal is rejected, then dt + β t Rt ( x; v, πt ) > 0 and                       19

20   dt ≤ dt + ζ t . Hence                                                                                          20

21                                                                                                                  21

22
                         Qt (ht+1 ; x, πt ) < Qt (ht+1 ; x, v) + ζ t + β t Rt ( x; v, πt ).                         22

23                                                                                                                  23
     Comparison with each feasible a and another use of the action certificate yield an
24                                                                                                                  24
     excess over Tt Jtπ+1 of at most αt + ζ t + 2β t χt . This common upper bound covers
25                                                                                                                  25
     acceptance as well. Since future costs weakly decrease,
26                                                                                                                  26
                                   +         +    +         +
27                                                                                                                  27
                                Jtπ = Ttπ Jtπ+1 ≤ Ttπ Jtπ+1 ≤ Tt Jtπ+1 + ϵ̃t .
28                                                                                                                  28

29   Monotonicity and the β t supremum-norm Lipschitz property of Tt give the asserted                              29

30   recursion. The terminal gap is zero, and repeated substitution along successive                                30

31   passes gives the stated finite-horizon unrolling. Every retained policy is feasible for                        31

32   the original optimum, so a zero upper bound implies equality with that optimum. 32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                               75

1    A fixed information contract may preclude zero greedy-search error; the theorem                                  1

2    does not assume it away.                                                                                         2

3      For sharpness take one period, two actions leading to distinct deterministic                                   3

4    terminal states, zero current cost, and β = 1. For w > 0 and 0 < δ < w, let the                                  4

5    terminal critic be (w, δ) and the terminal cost be (2w, δ). The incumbent selects the                            5

6    first state and the exact greedy proposal the second. The exact action-error contrast                            6

7    is w, so χ = w. The gate rejects because the critic contrast plus its allowance is δ > 0. 7
8    The retained policy’s gap is 2w − δ, whereas the terminal optimality gap and both                                8

9    search and arithmetic errors are zero. Letting δ decrease to zero excludes every                                 9

10   universal coefficient below two.                                                                      Q.E.D.     10

11                                                                                                                    11
       P ROOF OF P ROPOSITION 31:             The terminal assertion follows from eT = r T . At
12                                                                                         π (x)                      12
     an earlier date, the policy-evaluation identity is et ( x ) = rt ( x ) + β t Pt t              et+1 ( x ). The
13                                                                                                                    13
     coupling’s two marginals allow subtraction at states x, y inside one integral. The
14                                                                                                                    14
     triangle inequality and the inductive pair bound give
15                                                          Z                                                         15

16                 |et ( x ) − et (y)| ≤ Ht ( x, y) + β t       Dt+1 (u, v) dΛπ
                                                                              t ( x, y )( u, v ).                     16

17                                                                                                                    17
     The signed range supplies the separate bound wt , so the minimum is valid. Coupling
18                                                                                                                    18
     two action kernels at the same state gives the identical argument without the
19                                                                                                                    19
     residual term and establishes the action certificate. Replacing an integral by an upper
20                                                                                                                    20
     enclosure can only enlarge the bound before its intersection with the separately valid
21                                                                                                                    21
     scalar width. This argument uses the marginals, not independence or optimality of
22                                                                                                                    22
     the coupling.                                                                                         Q.E.D.
23                                                                                                                    23

24     P ROOF OF C OROLLARY 32:              The action difference annihilates n. Each of the                         24

25   two conditional means of u lies in its verified interval, hence their difference in                              25

26   absolute value is at most its width. Further, J π − (h + n) = u, so the existing signed- 26
27   residual recursion applied to the corrected critic yields the required interval without                          27

28   using an unknown true value. Adding n to the continuation changes every feasible                                 28

29   action objective by the same state-dependent amount; all action contrasts and                                    29

30   approximate-greedy inequalities are therefore unchanged. The conclusion applies                                  30

31   separately at each date and pass for which the membership and residual certificates                              31

32   are supplied.                                                                                         Q.E.D.     32
     76

1         P ROOF OF P ROPOSITION 33: In the original dynamics the coefficients of a in the       1

2    first two coordinates are 1/2 and 1/4. For each common innovation z, the coefficient        2

3    in F1 ( x, a, z) − 2F2 ( x, a, z) is consequently zero. Every function of that difference   3

4    has identical realized values for two feasible actions with the same z, and thus            4

5    identical conditional means under the original uniform innovation law. On the cube          5

6    y1 − 2y2 + 1 ranges from −1 to 2; its positive part ranges from zero to two. The            6

7    global terminal error width is exactly 2M. Its action contrast is nevertheless zero. 7
8    The corrected critic is the original terminal cost g, for which the evaluation error        8

9    vanishes. Proposal contrasts and their symbolic, outward-enclosed implementation            9

10   are unchanged. Keeping the same proposals, tie conventions and cellwise gate                10

11   therefore gives exactly the same terminal policy for every amplitude, not merely a          11

12   similar observed cost.                                                            Q.E.D.    12

13                                                                                               13

14                                                                                               14
                             33.1. Finite-state reference and limitations
15                                                                                               15

16     The exact reference implementation constructs a common-uniform coupling by                16

17   exhausting ordered probability masses. It verifies both marginals, propagates pair- 17
18   wise residual bounds backward, and forms every same-state action contrast. Its              18

19   regression suite independently evaluates the true finite-state policy values to test the    19

20   certificates; those true values are not used by the certificate constructor. Tests cover    20

21   one through five full improvement passes, a nonconstant action-null error with              21

22   amplitudes zero, one, sixteen and 4096, non-null errors, constant shifts, exact feasi- 22
23   bility, acceptance and rejection, and the sharp two-action example. These finite-state      23

24   programs validate executable identities and counterexamples; they do not discretize         24

25   the investment benchmark or establish full-horizon empirical convergence in that            25

26   continuous economy.                                                                         26

                                37. RECONSTRUCTION AND PRESERVATION
27                                                                                                                 27

28      The release verifies frozen science-source identities, every recorded checkpoint,                          28

29   all signed policy formulas, every first crossing, complete-prefix counts, repeated                            29

30   policy identities and the confidence intervals reconstructed from endpoint moments. 30
31   Additional exact tests check the primitive regions, resource allocation, original- 31
32   owner identity, fee semantics and net-choice bound. The complete development                                  32
     88

1    article and supplement retain all baseline labeled results; the original paths and      1

2    adverse results are unchanged. A clean Git-archive rebuild removes generated            2

3    PDFs and derived tables, then rebuilds from ordinary committed sources and              3

4    frozen records without network or new scientific execution. Compilation, finite tests   4

5    and source identities do not constitute independent mathematical peer approval.         5

           39. NUMERICAL CORRECTION AND SEQUENTIAL COMMON-ACCURACY DESIGN
23                                                                                                            23

24     The primary shared allocation is sufficient for the witness bound, not automati- 24
25   cally for conventional FVI’s actual interpolated modulus. Its failed certificates are                    25

26   retained. The common-accuracy amendment was specified after that outcome and                             26

27   frozen before its own execution; it charges every failed construction before doubling                    27

28   the state quota. It is not a retrospective recoding of the primary study.                                28

29     During publication review, nearest-rounded subtraction of different policies’ 29
30   already outward path endpoints was replaced by directed outward subtraction. The                         30

31   same bin streams, policies and sample counts were replayed. Original sources and                         31

32   results remain unchanged; a separate correction manifest binds the new evaluator,                        32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                     101

1    and the audit checks identical streams and unchanged sign classifications. Replaying                   1

2    the same observations adds no statistical sample size. The additive raw prefix field                   2

3    for coefficient-bit descriptors is not interpreted as a maximum; maximum bit length                    3

4    and live storage are reconstructed separately. The correction and development logs                     4

5    are part of the release.                                                                               5

                          41. DIRECTED FULL-SWEEP VERIFICATION RECORDS
25                                                                                                          25

26     The new main theorem is proved in the article. This supplement records the                           26

27   numerical objects to which it is applied. The scientific implementation and both                       27

28   source freezes are retained from the R53 execution. The primary services reconstruct                   28

29   the original two-state checkpoints from primitive costs, transitions, action menus                     29

30   and integration rules before building acquired actors. All subsequent policies are                     30

31   integer arrays of downward action indices. Their complete byte identities, rather                      31

32   than only their expected costs, determine whether a later pass has changed a policy. 32
     102

1      For every date and pass, the raw cell archive contains nine candidate lower and      1

2    upper advantage endpoints, the candidate quantum indices, eight lower-cover            2

3    intervals, the selected action, the accepted upper endpoint, the lower bound on        3

4    the continuous-action infimum and the directed greedy-gap upper endpoint. The          4

5    publication replay reconstructs the strict incumbent-preserving selection and com- 5
6    pares it with the deployed array. It also recomputes the maximum directed gap          6

7    and the exact rational finite-sweep recursion. Feasibility is checked using the same   7

8    complete-cell lower-capacity integer formula, independently of a displayed decimal     8

9    capacity.                                                                              9

11                        41.1. What the verification gauge computes                        11

12                                                                                          12
       The choice h = 0 makes the residual at an intermediate date equal to the incum-
13                                                                                          13
     bent stage cost and the terminal residual equal to g. Thus a pairwise continuation
14                                                                                          14
     calculation is a genuine evaluation of policy-value differences from the primitives.
15                                                                                          15
     Its residual-difference enclosures are generally nonconstant and its continuation-
16                                                                                          16
     contrast allowances generally positive. A signed interval for the advantage is more
17                                                                                          17
     informative than replacing that interval by its absolute maximum before deciding
18                                                                                          18
     whether an action helps. The symmetric and scalar candidate counts in the table are
19                                                                                          19
     computed on exactly the same menus and whole cells.
20                                                                                          20
       The inherited code also records the largest absolute stage-residual and propagated
21                                                                                          21
     pair endpoints visited by the recursion. These are diagnostics of the visited boxes,
22                                                                                          22
     not a claim that a single sampled number is the globally defined function Ht or Dt .
23                                                                                          23
     The certified functions are supplied by the enclosure procedure on any requested
24                                                                                          24
     state-pair box and are used on all boxes needed by the exhaustive root-cell and
25                                                                                          25
     action-cover calculation. Neither smoothness nor interpolation of the discontinuous
26                                                                                          26
     actor is assumed. Future rectangle extrema include every intersected acquired cell,
27                                                                                          27
     with both closed endpoints included.
28                                                                                          28

                              42. COMPLETE WORK AND STORAGE RECORDS
17                                                                                                                  17

18      The service record distinguishes primitive construction, acquired-actor prepa- 18
19   ration, each pass, serialized endpoint arrays and the final durable record. The                                19

20   shared path-cost service has its own clock, policy identities, sample stream and                               20

21   endpoint archive. The catalogue table in the article conservatively charges every                              21

22   own executed pass and the full shared inference service. Postprocessing, replay,                               22

23   typography checks and clean-archive reproduction have separate measured clocks                                 23

24   in the publication record; they are not silently assigned zero cost.                                           24

25      The logical actor-table count differs from total process memory. At five bits the                           25

26   exact two-dimensional minimum and maximum sparse tables require 149,760 bytes                                  26

27   per actor, including its action array and query-log array. The process also stores                             27

28   interval batches, policies, retained outputs, interpreter state and numerical-library                          28

29   workspaces. Its reported peak resident set therefore exceeds the actor-table count. 29
30   The pair-node count in the table is the final pass count; every earlier pass has a                             30

31   separate raw work record and must be included when summing total verification                                  31

32   operations.                                                                                                    32
     106

1      Adaptive refinement is paid for at every attempted construction rung, including        1

2    pilots. The surplus-driven comparator’s recorded date models are nonuniform              2

3    and its full adaptation history remains in its service JSON. A tensor layout with        3

4    unequal coordinate spacings is still a tensor layout. Neither this storage account nor   4

5    the common-innovation recursion is evidence for a non-tensor high-dimensional            5

6    runtime claim.                                                                           6

              43. APPROXIMATELY NULL COEFFICIENTS AND CONTROLLED ERRORS
8                                                                                             8

9      Let the perturbed action exposures be                                                  9

10                                                                                            10
                                 1                    1 ξ
11                           b1 = + ξ + λ,        b2 = − − λ,                                 11
                                 2                    4 2
12                                                                                            12
     and the ReLU direction be v = (1, −2 − η ). The exact action coefficient is
13                                                                                            13

14                                                        η ηξ                                14
                        s = b1 − (2 + η )b2 = 2ξ + 3λ −     +   + ηλ.
15
                                                          4   2                               15

16   For a common innovation, the one-Lipschitz property gives the verified continu- 16
17   ation allowance | Ms| | a − b|. The gate adds its discounted upper enclosure. Zero       17

18   is used only after the coefficient is checked exactly. The parameter λ is a known        18

19   action-dependent shift of the innovation mean, represented in the actual perturbed       19

20   kernel; it is not a test that an arbitrary unknown coupling has the correct marginals. 20
21     For each of the 384 cases, the raw archive retains the true-cost interval, nuisance    21

22   interval, allowance, critic upper bound and corrected upper bound for every candi- 22
23   date. It also retains both the safe selected policy and the deliberately uncorrected     23

24   false-null policy. The replay reconstructs the choices, verifies their identities and    24

25   counts strictly harmful false-null actions only when their true-cost lower endpoint      25

26   is positive. The primary economic experiment remains at the original unperturbed         26

27   law. Repetition of one cell under different amplitudes or perturbations is not a new     27

28   independent policy-cost observation.                                                     28

29                                                                                            29
                      44. COST INFERENCE, SELECTION, AND RELEASE SCOPE
30                                                                                            30

31     All marginal cost and paired difference intervals are recomputed from the de- 31
32   posited outward path endpoints using the frozen bounded-sample formula. Raw              32
                         NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                         107

1    path gains are allowed to be negative: conditional-expectation policy improvement       1

2    does not imply pathwise dominance under a common shock. The support used                2

3    for a paired gain remains the signed support [− H, H ], rather than clipping away       3

4    adverse paths. Identical policy arrays have exact zero contrasts and are recorded as    4

5    identities.                                                                             5

6      Selecting a pass with the smallest upper endpoint is valid on the simultaneous        6

7    event covering the frozen finite catalogue. It does not permit new policies, un- 7
8    declared initial laws or additional estimands without a corresponding coverage          8

9    account. The two R53 statistical families remain separate, and the R54 replay adds      9

10   no sample size. The pseudorandom seed fixes reproducibility; independence is the        10

11   statistical model, not a theorem about the seed.                                        11

12     The release preserves every inherited labeled result in the active and complete       12

13   documents and verifies unchanged bytes for historical evidence and frozen scientific    13

14   sources. It compiles the main article, supplement, response and both complete           14

15   editions, checks references and typography, and rebuilds from a clean archive           15

16   without scientific reruns. Compilation, finite tests and source identities are not an   16

17   external mathematical acceptance decision. The original broader NBO applications        17

18   retain their own hypotheses; the new linear action-kernel identities concern the        18

19   expected-cost model actually stated in the article.                                     19

20                                                                                           20
               45. EXECUTION, NUMERICAL SEMANTICS AND COMPLETE RECORDS
21                                                                                           21

22     The active article retains the constructive own-future NBO operator and adds          22

23   trained neural action search, non-tensor verification, prospective economic stopping    23

24   and validation-qualified learned structure. Its mathematical statements are proved      24

25   in the article. This supplement records the execution contract and all service out- 25
26   comes. The complete development and proof companions retain every preceding             26

27   theorem, application and adverse experiment under its original assumptions.             27

28                                                                                           28

29
                               45.1. Source and outcome identity                             29

30     The controlling advisory report reviews manuscript commit 00e837adb431f4d4            30

31   b5248fc6d5ff1fc927dd3b65. Its review commit is adf1256cff9cde365246a3db2da              31

32   c90c72fda3b13. The separately frozen primary and signed-sensitivity executions          32
     108

1    are inherited from 7f4de488134e93b6cfb5dd95a3e6d7e14b45ac2a. This manuscript           1

2    integration does not rerun those production services or add cost samples. The          2

3    source freezes, interrupted controller, corrected controller, fitted parameters, raw   3

4    endpoints, clocks and immutable process checkpoints remain unchanged.                  4

5      The publication audit reloads each saved fitted critic, reconstructs its proposal,   5

6    and regenerates each unique whole-domain certificate with the frozen numerical al- 6
7    gorithms. Every saved candidate endpoint, covering endpoint, residual band, value      7

8    band and policy choice is compared with that calculation. Repetition-equivalent        8

9    inputs are computed once and reused only after their canonical input identities        9

10   match. This is primitive re-evaluation of the recorded certificate with the same       10

11   algorithm, not an independent proof assistant or an independent implementation         11

12   of the entire economic simulator.                                                      12

13     All 132 services are then checked for their exact incumbent transfer, nine common    13

14   candidates and tenth proposal, complete continuous-action cover, strict minimum- 14
15   upper selection, feasibility, exact finite-sweep recurrence, declared look order, cu- 15
16   mulative sample prefixes and first legal stopping decision. Every referenced file      16

17   and process-log hash is checked. No later inference or construction is permitted       17

18   after a target crossing. A finite budget exhaustion remains in the outcome table.      18

19                                                                                          19

20                                                                                          20
                           45.2. Cross-platform moment verification
21                                                                                          21

22     Different numerical-library versions can differ in the final rounding of a sample    22

23   second moment even when their endpoint arrays are byte identical. The audit            23

24   therefore does not overwrite a scientific interval with a newly rounded interval       24

25   and call it the same record. Instead, it converts each binary64 endpoint to an exact   25

26   rational, computes its exact sample mean, second moment and unbiased variance,         26

27   and checks their containment in the originally stored outward moment account. It       27

28   reconstructs the rational empirical-Bernstein endpoints from those stored accounts. 28
29   Thus a valid original interval can be verified without assuming bit identity of a      29

30   library reduction on another platform.                                                 30

31     Validation confidence boxes receive the corresponding exact-mean check, with         31

32   their stored confidence radius and numerical-bin allowance. All policy-cost end- 32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           109

1    points retain signed path gains. Conditional expected-cost safety does not imply          1

2    that a common-shock pathwise gain is nonnegative; such paths are not clipped              2

3    away. The declared independent-bin statistical model remains distinct from the            3

4    reproducibility of a pseudorandom seed.                                                   4

5                                                                                              5

6                                                                                              6
                              45.3. Residual cache and signed tubes
7                                                                                              7

8      A range query includes every closed leaf meeting the query box. This is conser- 8
9    vative at a boundary but preserves the half-open deployed observation rule. No            9

10   smoothness is imposed on the actor. The first-stage installed policy is globally zero. 10
11   Sensitivity is used only under that verified reference identity; a changed incumbent      11

12   falls back to the generic cache. The forward zero-policy tube contains the entire         12

13   future innovation support and the backward tube uses the sparse Jacobian of the           13

14   original drift. Its first innovation is integrated over the declared bins with exact      14

15   probabilities. The algorithm does not recursively enumerate all q T future bins.          15

16     Each raw candidate and covering comparison retains its base-cache interval,             16

17   signed-tube interval and intersection. Empty intersections are errors, not oppor- 17
18   tunities to choose a midpoint. Table entries for U − C and C − L are maxima of            18

19   independently nonnegative cellwise quantities. Their separate maxima need not             19

20   sum to the displayed maximum of U − L. Refinement attribution is a comparison             20

21   of valid enclosures, not a causal decomposition of state discretization, action search,   21

22   stochastic integration and floating-point error.                                          22

23                                                                                             23

24                                                                                             24
                                 45.4. Exact neural action search
25                                                                                             25

26     The new scalar-action solver reads the exact stored dyadic coefficients. It enumer- 26
27   ates rational hinge breakpoints, constructs the cubic derivative of each quartic piece,   27

28   isolates every real stationary root by rational intervals, and compares candidate         28

29   lattice actions by exact rational objective values. Multiple roots and zero innovation    29

30   projections have explicit tests. The retained saved-model regressions specify one         30

31   acquired-cell center in each task before checking its entire finite lattice exhaustively. 31
32   They create neither a replacement production policy nor a new economic observa- 32
     110

1    tion. Root-isolation cost and coefficient bit lengths are not replaced by a unit-cost   1

2    count in a runtime claim.                                                               2

17                                                                                             17
                              49. READING AND PRESERVATION MAP
18                                                                                             18

19     The active article is organized around construction and training, acquired-state        19

20   improvement, prospective stopping and actual economic cost. The preceding main            20

21   article is retained as an unchanged historical source and a rebuilt development           21

22   document. The complete development article and complete proof supplement retain           22

23   the full prior body and add the current theoretical and numerical sections. Historical    23

24   labels and immutable section-source hashes are checked rather than relying on a           24

25   statement that no results were removed.                                                   25

26     The original resource-allocation, original-owner compiler, observation-price,           26

27   recursive-preference, diffusion, temporal-self and game results remain available          27

28   in those companions. The expected-cost sampling and linear action-kernel results          28

29   in the active article do not claim that a nonlinear recursive utility or game has         29

30   been experimentally validated by an investment-policy interval. A reading map is          30

31   a distinction among assumptions and evidence, not abandonment of the original             31

32   NBO research object.                                                                      32
     124

1      50. EXECUTED ACTION-SEARCH SERVICES AND COMPLETE NUMERICAL ACCOUNTS                  1

2                                                                                           2
       This section records the new R57 execution, separately from the preceding R55
3                                                                                           3
     cohorts and their R56 integration. Its scientific protocol, executable source and
4                                                                                           4
     declared target family were frozen before production. The publication replay does
5                                                                                           5
     not retrain the critics, choose new seeds, draw new economic observations or replace
6                                                                                           6
     the original service clocks.
7                                                                                           7

8                                                                                           8
                            50.1. Exact search and proposal identity
9                                                                                           9
       For each saved critic, the replay reconstructs the integrated action objective
10                                                                                          10
     from the exact stored dyadic coefficients. The action-dependent objective drops
11                                                                                          11
     only a common additive constant. It recomputes rational hinge breakpoints, cubic
12                                                                                          12
     stationary-root isolation and exact rational comparisons on the action lattice. It
13                                                                                          13
     checks the chosen index, objective value, nonterminal menu optimization loss and
14                                                                                          14
     root/piece counts. Different valid rational isolating brackets are not by themselves
15                                                                                          15
     different economic actions; the selected lattice index and exact objective are the
16                                                                                          16
     controlling solver identities.
17                                                                                          17
       The ReLU-exact mode keeps every ReLU-menu candidate. The quadratic mode
18                                                                                          18
     has its own exact fitted-value search; the other modes retain their declared menu
19                                                                                          19
     construction. Every terminal proposal uses the same analytic terminal objective
20                                                                                          20
     under the original law. The whole-cell signed reference verifier is independent of
21                                                                                          21
     the critic, so a shared action receives identical endpoints in the matched search
22                                                                                          22
     comparison. The replay reconstructs all raw candidate arrays, selected actions,
23                                                                                          23
     continuous-action lower covers and datewise gaps. Removing the added algebraic
24                                                                                          24
     action uses the stored pre-addition minimum and policy; no different training run
25                                                                                          25
     is substituted as the counterfactual menu.

27   original Bellman optimum, including the old-continuation term, not merely the local greedy gap. The   27

28   expected-cost target and this conservative uniform bound are different quantities.   Each service     28

29   is a separate from-primitives process with its own inference and durable output. 29
30   Within a service, resource refinement restarts from the same installed reference,                     30

31   and no intermediate candidate is deployed. A target-attained return requires a                        31

32   nonpositive confidence upper bound on the signed target estimand. A budget- 32
     132

1    exhausted return carries a statewise-safe candidate but no attained-target claim. 1
2    The registry preserves the complete declared set rather than selecting successful     2

3    seeds.                                                                                3

4      The all-state column uses the support-capped one-sweep recursion against the        4

5    original Bellman optimum. It is not the directed greedy gap alone. The old continu- 5
6    ation error remains part of the account even when every selected action is verified   6

7    nonworsening. The exact finite-sweep theorem retains its own premises; two re- 7
8    source refinements against a fixed installed reference are not two simultaneous       8

9    policy-iteration passes against successive incumbents.                                9

23                                                                                                              23

24                                                                                                              24

25     Each nonidentity interval comes from 65,536 fresh continuous-bin paths for the five returned policies. 25
26   Negative values favor X. Full raw endpoint arrays, stream hashes, fitted models and prospective stopping   26

27   records are deposited. The validation stream is generated only after the corresponding                     27

28   target services have returned. The replay reconstructs its original forty-bit initial- 28
29   state and innovation bins and recomputes every path-cost enclosure. It likewise                            29

30   reconstructs every original stopping stream and every declared look, including                             30

31   unsuccessful early looks. Replaying the same frozen stream is verification, not an                         31

32   increase in sample size.                                                                                   32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                         135

1      Original confidence records are verified using exact rational first and second          1

2    sample moments and the inherited rounded-moment bounds. This separates an                 2

3    arithmetic guarantee from platform-dependent floating-point reduction order. The          3

4    support for a signed difference includes negative values. Statewise expected safety       4

5    relative to the installed policy is not used to clip common-shock path gains. Exact       5

6    policy identities and unresolved statistical differences are retained as different cases. 6
7      The new confidence family bounds all declared stopping and paired intervals             7

8    simultaneously. It is not a claim of independence across methods, targets or reused       8

9    policy arrays. The three different training seeds generate different fitted objects;      9

10   they do not establish an optimizer-success distribution or generalization across an       10

11   unobserved population of economic environments.                                           11

12                                                                                             12

32                                                                                             32
     136

1      Components sum within a run, but their separate medians need not sum to the median complete               1

2    process time. They exclude some serialization and process overhead that is included in the complete         2

3    clock. Bytes and RSS are maxima across the three runs. Cross-task runners are not treated as a controlled   3

4    hardware-scaling regression. The complete own-service clock surrounds the subprocess                        4

5    through termination and durable service records. Internal components separately                             5

6    measure fitting, algebraic search, safety verification and inference through endpoint                       6

7    serialization. Exact rational search pays for its coefficient arithmetic, stationary- 7
8    root isolation and candidate comparisons. Logical operation counts do not replace                           8

9    the measured process cost, and process seconds are not claimed to be machine                                9

10   independent.                                                                                                10

11     The supplementary paired-comparison timer is narrower: it ends after endpoint                             11

12   computation and serialization, before the final comparison JSON is committed. 12
13   The driver executes this comparison in its parent process. Thus the intended sep- 13
14   arate complete comparison-process timer in the protocol was not obtained. The                               14

15   recorded value is a partial, additional study-overhead measurement, not a complete                          15

16   deployment clock. All own-target-service clocks remain complete. The publication                            16

17   preserves and labels the narrower timing rather than inventing an unobserved                                17

18   overhead or silently amending the raw record.                                                               18

18     This printed subset uses the first prespecified seed and the twenty-percent target for all modes. Every   18

19   seed and target is in the machine-readable audit. Each numerical entry is a maximum over leaves; 19
20   maxima of the first two components need not add to the maximum total gap.            The displayed          20

21   first-seed subset is specified mechanically, not chosen for favorable outcomes. Full                        21

22   datewise arrays for every seed, target and attempted policy remain in the machine- 22
23   readable replay and raw records. For each leaf the identity (U − L) = (U − C ) + 23
24   (C − L) is exact over the recorded endpoints; printed entries are separate outward- 24
25   rounded maxima. Acquisition, innovation and arithmetic effects can contribute                               25

26   to both components. The analytic fitted-feature moduli in the article supply an                             26

27   additional primitive account and are not misreported as a separately executed                               27

28   six-factor numerical experiment.                                                                            28

29     The R57 article adds the fitted-witness transfer theorem, its action-sensitive neu- 29
30   ral moduli and the new prospective experiment to the same NBO manuscript. The                               30

31   full preceding article, supplement, response, broader applications and unfavorable                          31

32   comparisons remain in the preserved and complete development documents. The                                 32
